Prompt
请解答以下数学题（单选题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

1. 样本数据6,8,4,5,12的中位数为（ ）
A. 5
B. 6
C. 8
D. 9

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
要计算一组数据的中位数，需按以下步骤进行：

1. **数据排序**：首先将样本数据按从小到大的顺序重新排列。
   原数据：$6, 8, 4, 5, 12$
   排序后：$4, 5, 6, 8, 12$

2. **确定中位数位置**：该组数据共有 $n=5$ 个（奇数个）。当中位数的位置为 $\frac{n+1}{2} = \frac{5+1}{2} = 3$，即排在正中间的第3个数。

3. **找出对应数值**：排序后的第3个数是 $6$，因此该组数据的中位数为 $6$。

对照选项，B选项正确。

\boxed{B}

     - Original: 6, 8, 4, 5, 12

gaokao_000_001 ✓ A A 1972 128.5 15.3 http://192.168.0.41:9292
Prompt
请解答以下数学题（单选题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

2. 已知平面向量\vec{a}，\vec{b}不共线，且2\vec{a} + y\vec{b} = x\vec{a} - 3\vec{b}，则（ ）
A. x=2, y=-3
B. x=-2, y=3
C. x=2, y=3
D. x=-2, y=-3

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
**逐步推理：**

1. **理解已知条件**：题目给出平面向量 $\vec{a}$ 与 $\vec{b}$ **不共线**。在平面几何中，不共线的两个向量线性无关，可以作为该平面的一组基底。
2. **应用平面向量基本定理**：根据该定理，若 $\vec{a}, \vec{b}$ 不共线，则对于任意向量，其表示为 $m\vec{a} + n\vec{b}$ 的形式时，系数 $m$ 和 $n$ 是**唯一确定**的。也就是说，若 $m_1\vec{a} + n_1\vec{b} = m_2\vec{a} + n_2\vec{b}$，则必然有 $m_1 = m_2$ 且 $n_1 = n_2$。
3. **对比系数求解**：
   给定等式为：$2\vec{a} + y\vec{b} = x\vec{a} - 3\vec{b}$
   由于 $\vec{a}, \vec{b}$ 不共线，等式两边同向量的对应系数必须相等：
   - $\vec{a}$ 的系数：$x = 2$
   - $\vec{b}$ 的系数：$y = -3$
4. **对照选项**：解得 $x=2, y=-3$，与选项 A 完全一致。

**最终答案：**
\boxed{A}
Reasoning
Here's a thinking process:

1.  **Analyze User Input:**
   - **Question Type:** Multiple choice question (单选题)
   - **Given:** Plane vectors $\vec{a}$ and $\vec{b}$ are not collinear (不共线).
   - **Equation:** $2\vec{a} + y\vec{b} = x\vec{a} - 3\vec{b}$
   - **Goal:** Find the values of $x$ and $y$.
   - **Options:**
     A. $x=2, y=-3$
     B. $x=-2, y=3$
     C. $x=2, y=3$
     D. $x=-2, y=-3$

2.  **Identify Key Mathematical Concepts:**
   - Vectors $\vec{a}$ and $\vec{b}$ are non-collinear (linearly independent) in a plane.
   - The fundamental theorem of plane vectors (平面向量基本定理) states that if $\vec{a}$ and $\vec{b}$ are non-collinear, then any vector $\vec{c}$ in the plane can be uniquely expressed as $\vec{c} = m\vec{a} + n\vec{b}$, where $m$ and $n$ are unique scalars.
   - More specifically, if $m_1\vec{a} + n_1\vec{b} = m_2\vec{a} + n_2\vec{b}$ and $\vec{a}, \vec{b}$ are non-collinear, then by linear independence, $m_1 = m_2$ and $n_1 = n_2$.

3.  **Apply Concepts to the Problem:**
   - Given equation: $2\vec{a} + y\vec{b} = x\vec{a} - 3\vec{b}$
   - Rearrange or compare coefficients of $\vec{a}$ and $\vec{b}$ on both sides.
   - Coefficient of $\vec{a}$ on LHS: 2
   - Coefficient of $\vec{a}$ on RHS: $x$
   - Coefficient of $\vec{b}$ on LHS: $y$
   - Coefficient of $\vec{b}$ on RHS: $-3$
   - Since $\vec{a}$ and $\vec{b}$ are non-collinear (linearly independent), the coefficients must be equal:
     $x = 2$
     $y = -3$

4.  **Match with Options:**
   - Found: $x=2, y=-3$
   - Option A matches exactly.

5.  **Formulate Response (Internal Refinement):**
   - State the given condition: $\vec{a}, \vec{b}$ are non-collinear.
   - Quote the principle: According to the uniqueness of vector representation (or linear independence), corresponding coefficients must be equal.
   - Set up equations: $x = 2$, $y = -3$.
   - Conclude with the correct option.
   - Format properly with `\boxed{}` as requested.

   Structure:
   - 逐步推理过程
   - 明确指出平面向量基本定理/线性无关性质
   - 得出x和y的值
   - 对照选项
   - 最终答案放入boxed

gaokao_000_002 ✓ C C 2616 155.8 16.8 http://192.168.0.41:9292
Prompt
请解答以下数学题（单选题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

3. 已知集合A={sin\frac{7\pi}{6}, cos\frac{5\pi}{3}, tan\frac{5\pi}{4}}, B={-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, -\frac{1}{2}, 1}，则A \cap B = （ ）
A. {-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, -\frac{1}{2}}
B. {-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, 1}
C. {-\frac{1}{2}, 1}
D. {-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, -\frac{1}{2}, 1}

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
**逐步推理：**

1. **计算集合 \(A\) 中各三角函数的值：**
   - \(\sin\frac{7\pi}{6} = \sin\left(\pi + \frac{\pi}{6}\right) = -\sin\frac{\pi}{6} = -\frac{1}{2}\)
   - \(\cos\frac{5\pi}{3} = \cos\left(2\pi - \frac{\pi}{3}\right) = \cos\frac{\pi}{3} = \frac{1}{2}\)
   - \(\tan\frac{5\pi}{4} = \tan\left(\pi + \frac{\pi}{4}\right) = \tan\frac{\pi}{4} = 1\)
   
   因此，集合 \(A = \left\{-\frac{1}{2}, \frac{1}{2}, 1\right\}\)。

2. **明确集合 \(B\)：**
   \(B = \left\{-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, -\frac{1}{2}, 1\right\}\)。

3. **求交集 \(A \cap B\)：**
   比较集合 \(A\) 和 \(B\) 的元素，找出公共部分：
   - \(-\frac{1}{2}\) 同时存在于 \(A\) 和 \(B\) 中；
   - \(1\) 同时存在于 \(A\) 和 \(B\) 中；
   - \(\frac{1}{2}\) 仅在 \(A\) 中，\(-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\) 仅在 \(B\) 中。
   
   所以，\(A \cap B = \left\{-\frac{1}{2}, 1\right\}\)。

4. **对照选项：**
   该结果与选项 **C** 完全一致。

\boxed{C}
Reasoning
Here's a thinking process:

1.  **Analyze User Input:**
   - **Question:** A multiple-choice question about set intersection.
   - **Set A:** $A = \{\sin\frac{7\pi}{6}, \cos\frac{5\pi}{3}, \tan\frac{5\pi}{4}\}$
   - **Set B:** $B = \{-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, -\frac{1}{2}, 1\}$
   - **Goal:** Find $A \cap B$ (the intersection of sets A and B).
   - **Options:**
     - A. $\{-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, -\frac{1}{2}\}$
     - B. $\{-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, 1\}$
     - C. $\{-\frac{1}{2}, 1\}$
     - D. $\{-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, -\frac{1}{2}, 1\}$

2.  **Evaluate Elements of Set A:**
   - Calculate $\sin\frac{7\pi}{6}$:
     - $\frac{7\pi}{6} = \pi + \frac{\pi}{6}$
     - $\sin(\pi + \theta) = -\sin\theta$
     - $\sin\frac{\pi}{6} = \frac{1}{2}$
     - So, $\sin\frac{7\pi}{6} = -\frac{1}{2}$
   - Calculate $\cos\frac{5\pi}{3}$:
     - $\frac{5\pi}{3} = 2\pi - \frac{\pi}{3}$
     - $\cos(2\pi - \theta) = \cos\theta$
     - $\cos\frac{\pi}{3} = \frac{1}{2}$
     - So, $\cos\frac{5\pi}{3} = \frac{1}{2}$
   - Calculate $\tan\frac{5\pi}{4}$:
     - $\frac{5\pi}{4} = \pi + \frac{\pi}{4}$
     - $\tan(\pi + \theta) = \tan\theta$
     - $\tan\frac{\pi}{4} = 1$
     - So, $\tan\frac{5\pi}{4} = 1$
   - Therefore, $A = \{-\frac{1}{2}, \frac{1}{2}, 1\}$

3.  **Compare Set A and Set B:**
   - $A = \{-\frac{1}{2}, \frac{1}{2}, 1\}$
   - $B = \{-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, -\frac{1}{2}, 1\}$
   - Find common elements (intersection $A \cap B$):
     - $-\frac{1}{2}$ is in both A and B.
     - $\frac{1}{2}$ is in A but not in B.
     - $1$ is in both A and B.
     - $-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}$ is in B but not in A.
   - So, $A \cap B = \{-\frac{1}{2}, 1\}$

4.  **Match with Options:**
   - A. $\{-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, -\frac{1}{2}\}$ -> Incorrect
   - B. $\{-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, 1\}$ -> Incorrect
   - C. $\{-\frac{1}{2}, 1\}$ -> Correct
   - D. $\{-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, -\frac{1}{2}, 1\}$ -> Incorrect

5.  **Double-check Calculations:**
   - $\sin(210^\circ) = -0.5$ -> Correct.
   - $\cos(300^\circ) = \cos(360^\circ - 60^\circ) = \cos(60^\circ) = 0.5$ -> Correct.
   - $\tan(225^\circ) = \tan(180^\circ + 45^\circ) = \tan(45^\circ) = 1$ -> Correct.
   - $A = \{-0.5, 0.5, 1\}$
   - $B = \{-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} \approx -0.866, -0.5, 1\}$
   - Intersection: $\{-0.5, 1\}$
   - Matches Option C.

   All steps are solid. No pitfalls found. The question is straightforward trigonometric evaluation and set intersection.

6.  **Formulate Response:**
   - State the evaluation of each element in set A.
   - List set A explicitly.
   - Compare with set B.
   - Determine the intersection.
   - Match with the given options.
   - Provide the final answer in the requested format.
   - Structure: Step-by-step reasoning, then final answer in \boxed{}.
   - Language: Chinese (as requested).

   Draft:
   首先计算集合A中各三角函数的值：
   $\sin\frac{7\pi}{6} = \sin(\pi + \frac{\pi}{6}) = -\sin\frac{\pi}{6} = -\frac{1}{2}$
   $\cos\frac{5\pi}{3} = \cos(2\pi - \frac{\pi}{3}) = \cos\frac{\pi}{3} = \frac{1}{2}$
   $\tan\frac{5\pi}{4} = \tan(\pi + \frac{\pi}{4}) = \tan\frac{\pi}{4} = 1$

   $1$ 在A和B中都有；
   $\frac{1}{2}$ 仅在A中，$-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}$ 仅在B中。
   因此 $A \cap B = \{-\frac{1}{2}, 1\}$。

gaokao_000_003 ✓ D D 2117 147.5 14.4 http://192.168.0.41:9292
Prompt
请解答以下数学题（单选题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

4. 曲线y=5x+8lnx在点(1,5)处的切线方程为（ ）
A. y=3x+2
B. y=5x
C. y=8x-3
D. y=13x-8

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
要求曲线 $y=5x+8\ln x$ 在点 $(1,5)$ 处的切线方程，可按以下步骤逐步求解：

1. **求导数（切线斜率函数）**：
   曲线在某点处的切线斜率等于该点处的导数值。对函数求导：
   $$y' = \frac{d}{dx}(5x) + \frac{d}{dx}(8\ln x) = 5 + \frac{8}{x}$$

2. **计算切点处的斜率**：
   将切点的横坐标 $x=1$ 代入导函数：
   $$k = y'(1) = 5 + \frac{8}{1} = 13$$
   即切线斜率为 $13$。

3. **写出切线方程**：
   利用直线的点斜式方程 $y - y_0 = k(x - x_0)$，代入切点 $(1, 5)$ 和斜率 $k=13$：
   $$y - 5 = 13(x - 1)$$

4. **化简整理**：
   $$y = 13x - 13 + 5$$
   $$y = 13x - 8$$

对比所给选项，该方程与选项 D 完全一致。

故正确答案为 \boxed{D}。
Reasoning
Here's a thinking process:

1.  **Understand the User's Request:**
   - **Problem:** Find the equation of the tangent line to the curve $y = 5x + 8\ln x$ at the point $(1, 5)$.
   - **Type:** Multiple choice question (single answer).
   - **Options:** 
     A. $y = 3x + 2$
     B. $y = 5x$
     C. $y = 8x - 3$
     D. $y = 13x - 8$
   - **Requirement:** Provide step-by-step reasoning and put the final answer in `\boxed{}`.

2.  **Identify Key Mathematical Concepts:**
   - Tangent line to a curve at a given point.
   - The slope of the tangent line at a point is given by the derivative of the function at that point.
   - Equation of a line: point-slope form $y - y_0 = m(x - x_0)$.
   - Derivative rules: $\frac{d}{dx}(ax) = a$, $\frac{d}{dx}(k \ln x) = \frac{k}{x}$.

3.  **Step-by-Step Solution:**
   - **Step 1: Verify the point lies on the curve.**
     $x = 1 \Rightarrow y = 5(1) + 8\ln(1) = 5 + 8(0) = 5$. So $(1, 5)$ is indeed on the curve.
   - **Step 2: Find the derivative $y'$ to get the slope of the tangent line.**
     $y = 5x + 8\ln x$
     $y' = \frac{d}{dx}(5x) + \frac{d}{dx}(8\ln x) = 5 + \frac{8}{x}$
   - **Step 3: Evaluate the derivative at $x = 1$ to find the slope $m$.**
     $m = y'(1) = 5 + \frac{8}{1} = 5 + 8 = 13$
   - **Step 4: Use the point-slope form to write the equation of the tangent line.**
     Point: $(x_0, y_0) = (1, 5)$
     Slope: $m = 13$
     Equation: $y - 5 = 13(x - 1)$
   - **Step 5: Simplify to slope-intercept form ($y = mx + b$).**
     $y - 5 = 13x - 13$
     $y = 13x - 13 + 5$
     $y = 13x - 8$
   - **Step 6: Match with the given options.**
     The derived equation $y = 13x - 8$ matches option D.

4.  **Double-Check:**
   - Derivative: $5 + 8/x$. At $x=1$, slope is 13. Correct.
   - Point-slope: $y - 5 = 13(x - 1) \Rightarrow y = 13x - 8$. Correct.
   - Options: A (3x+2), B (5x), C (8x-3), D (13x-8). Matches D.
   - All steps are solid. No tricks or hidden complexities. Domain of $\ln x$ is $x>0$, $x=1$ is valid.

Prompt
请解答以下数学题（单选题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

5. 已知抛物线C_1:y^2=2p_1x (p_1>0)和C_2:x^2=2p_2y (p_2>0)均经过点(4,8)，则C_1的焦点与C_2的焦点之间的距离为（ ）
A. 12
B. 4\sqrt{5}
C. 6
D. \frac{\sqrt{65}}{2}

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
**逐步推理过程：**

1. **求 $p_1$ 及抛物线 $C_1$ 的焦点坐标**
   已知抛物线 $C_1: y^2 = 2p_1x$ 经过点 $(4, 8)$，将点坐标代入方程：
   $$8^2 = 2p_1 \times 4 \Rightarrow 64 = 8p_1 \Rightarrow p_1 = 8$$
   抛物线 $y^2 = 2px\,(p>0)$ 的标准焦点坐标为 $(\frac{p}{2}, 0)$。
   故 $C_1$ 的焦点为 $F_1(\frac{8}{2}, 0) = (4, 0)$。

2. **求 $p_2$ 及抛物线 $C_2$ 的焦点坐标**
   已知抛物线 $C_2: x^2 = 2p_2y$ 经过点 $(4, 8)$，将点坐标代入方程：
   $$4^2 = 2p_2 \times 8 \Rightarrow 16 = 16p_2 \Rightarrow p_2 = 1$$
   抛物线 $x^2 = 2py\,(p>0)$ 的标准焦点坐标为 $(0, \frac{p}{2})$。
   故 $C_2$ 的焦点为 $F_2(0, \frac{1}{2})$。

3. **计算两焦点之间的距离**
   利用两点间距离公式计算 $F_1(4, 0)$ 与 $F_2(0, \frac{1}{2})$ 的距离：
   $$|F_1F_2| = \sqrt{(4 - 0)^2 + \left(0 - \frac{1}{2}\right)^2} = \sqrt{16 + \frac{1}{4}} = \sqrt{\frac{65}{4}} = \frac{\sqrt{65}}{2}$$

4. **对照选项**
   计算结果 $\frac{\sqrt{65}}{2}$ 与选项 D 完全一致。

最终答案为 $\boxed{D}$。
Reasoning
Here's a thinking process:

1.  **Analyze User Input:**
   - **Problem Type:** Multiple choice question (single answer)
   - **Given:** Two parabolas:
     - $C_1: y^2 = 2p_1x$ with $p_1 > 0$
     - $C_2: x^2 = 2p_2y$ with $p_2 > 0$
     - Both pass through the point $(4, 8)$
   - **Question:** Find the distance between the focus of $C_1$ and the focus of $C_2$.
   - **Options:** A. 12, B. $4\sqrt{5}$, C. 6, D. $\frac{\sqrt{65}}{2}$
   - **Output Requirement:** Step-by-step reasoning, final answer in $\boxed{}$.

   - Standard form of parabola $x^2 = 2py$ ($p>0$): Focus is at $(0, p/2)$.
   - Point on parabola satisfies its equation.
   - Distance between two points $(x_1, y_1)$ and $(x_2, y_2)$ is $\sqrt{(x_2-x_1)^2 + (y_2-y_1)^2}$.

3.  **Calculate $p_1$ and $p_2$:**

     $4^2 = 2p_2 \cdot 8 \Rightarrow 16 = 16p_2 \Rightarrow p_2 = 1$.

6. 已知函数f(x)=\frac{x+2}{e^x+a}的最大值为1，则a = （ ）

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
由题意，函数 $f(x)=\frac{x+2}{e^x+a}$ 的最大值为 $1$。我们逐步求解如下：

**第一步：求导并确定极值点条件**
对 $f(x)$ 求导（使用商的求导法则）：
$$f'(x) = \frac{(x+2)'(e^x+a) - (x+2)(e^x+a)'}{(e^x+a)^2} = \frac{1\cdot(e^x+a) - (x+2)e^x}{(e^x+a)^2} = \frac{a - e^x(x+1)}{(e^x+a)^2}$$
令 $f'(x)=0$，得 $a = e^x(x+1)$。设取得最大值的点为 $x_0$，则有：
$$a = e^{x_0}(x_0+1) \quad \text{......①}$$

**第二步：利用最大值为1的条件列方程**
因为 $f(x)$ 的最大值为 $1$，所以 $f(x_0)=1$，即：
$$\frac{x_0+2}{e^{x_0}+a} = 1 \Rightarrow x_0+2 = e^{x_0}+a \Rightarrow a = x_0+2-e^{x_0} \quad \text{......②}$$

**第三步：联立方程求解**
联立①②两式，消去参数 $a$：
$$e^{x_0}(x_0+1) = x_0+2-e^{x_0}$$
移项合并同类项：
$$e^{x_0}(x_0+1) + e^{x_0} = x_0+2$$
$$e^{x_0}(x_0+2) = x_0+2$$
若 $x_0+2=0$，即 $x_0=-2$，代入①式得 $a=e^{-2}(-1)<0$，与选项均为正数矛盾，舍去。
故 $x_0+2\neq 0$，方程两边同除以 $(x_0+2)$ 得：
$$e^{x_0}=1 \Rightarrow x_0=0$$
将 $x_0=0$ 代入①式（或②式），求得：
$$a = e^0(0+1) = 1$$

**第四步：验证**
当 $a=1$ 时，$f(x)=\frac{x+2}{e^x+1}$。此时 $f'(x)=\frac{1-e^x(x+1)}{(e^x+1)^2}$。
- 当 $x<0$ 时，$e^x(x+1)<1$，$f'(x)>0$，函数单调递增；
- 当 $x>0$ 时，$e^x(x+1)>1$，$f'(x)<0$，函数单调递减。
因此 $x=0$ 处取得极大值，也是全局最大值：$f(0)=\frac{0+2}{e^0+1}=1$，完全符合题意。

综上所述，$a=1$，对应选项 B。

最终答案：\boxed{B}
Reasoning
Here's a thinking process:

1.  **Understand the User Input:**
   - **Problem:** Given the function $f(x) = \frac{x+2}{e^x + a}$ has a maximum value of 1, find the value of $a$.
   - **Options:** A. $\frac{1}{2}$, B. 1, C. $\frac{3}{2}$, D. 2
   - **Format:** Single choice question. Provide step-by-step reasoning and put the final answer in $\boxed{}$.

2.  **Analyze the Function:**
   - $f(x) = \frac{x+2}{e^x + a}$
   - Domain: $e^x + a \neq 0$. Since $e^x > 0$, if $a \ge 0$, domain is $\mathbb{R}$. If $a < 0$, there's a vertical asymptote at $x = \ln(-a)$. The problem likely assumes $a > 0$ or at least such that the function is defined on an interval containing the maximum. Given the options are positive, assume $a > 0$.
   - We are told the maximum value is 1. This means $\max_{x} f(x) = 1$.
   - Let's find the critical points by taking the derivative $f'(x)$.

3.  **Compute Derivative:**
   - $f(x) = \frac{u}{v}$ where $u = x+2$, $v = e^x + a$.
   - $f'(x) = \frac{u'v - uv'}{v^2} = \frac{1 \cdot (e^x + a) - (x+2) \cdot e^x}{(e^x + a)^2}$
   - Simplify numerator: $e^x + a - (x+2)e^x = e^x + a - xe^x - 2e^x = a - e^x - xe^x = a - e^x(1+x)$
   - So, $f'(x) = \frac{a - e^x(x+1)}{(e^x + a)^2}$

4.  **Find Critical Points:**
   - Set $f'(x) = 0 \implies a - e^x(x+1) = 0 \implies e^x(x+1) = a$
   - Let $g(x) = e^x(x+1)$. We need to solve $g(x) = a$.
   - Analyze $g(x)$: $g'(x) = e^x(x+1) + e^x = e^x(x+2)$.
   - $g'(x) = 0 \implies x = -2$.
   - For $x < -2$, $g'(x) < 0$ (decreasing). For $x > -2$, $g'(x) > 0$ (increasing).
   - Minimum of $g(x)$ is at $x = -2$, $g(-2) = e^{-2}(-2+1) = -e^{-2}$.
   - Since $a > 0$ (from options), the equation $g(x) = a$ has exactly one real solution $x_0 > -2$.
   - At this critical point $x_0$, $f(x)$ has a local extremum. Since $f'(x) > 0$ for $x < x_0$ and $f'(x) < 0$ for $x > x_0$ (check signs: numerator is $a - g(x)$, which goes from positive to negative as $x$ increases through $x_0$), this critical point is a local maximum.
   - Since it's the only critical point and the function tends to 0 as $x \to \pm \infty$ (for $a>0$), this local maximum is indeed the global maximum.

5.  **Use the Maximum Value Condition:**
   - We are given $\max f(x) = 1$. So $f(x_0) = 1$.
   - $f(x_0) = \frac{x_0 + 2}{e^{x_0} + a} = 1 \implies x_0 + 2 = e^{x_0} + a$
   - We also have the critical point condition: $e^{x_0}(x_0 + 1) = a$
   - Now we have a system of two equations with variables $x_0$ and $a$:
     1. $a = e^{x_0}(x_0 + 1)$
     2. $a = x_0 + 2 - e^{x_0}$
   - Set them equal: $e^{x_0}(x_0 + 1) = x_0 + 2 - e^{x_0}$
   - Simplify: $e^{x_0}(x_0 + 1) + e^{x_0} = x_0 + 2$
   - $e^{x_0}(x_0 + 2) = x_0 + 2$
   - Case 1: $x_0 + 2 \neq 0 \implies e^{x_0} = 1 \implies x_0 = 0$.
   - Case 2: $x_0 + 2 = 0 \implies x_0 = -2$. But we know $x_0 > -2$ for $a>0$, and checking $x_0=-2$ gives $a = e^{-2}(-1) < 0$, contradicting $a>0$. So $x_0 = 0$ is the only valid solution.
   - Substitute $x_0 = 0$ into either equation for $a$:
     - $a = e^0(0 + 1) = 1$
     - $a = 0 + 2 - e^0 = 1$
   - So $a = 1$.

6.  **Verify:**
   - If $a = 1$, $f(x) = \frac{x+2}{e^x+1}$.
   - $f'(x) = \frac{1 - e^x(x+1)}{(e^x+1)^2}$.
   - $f'(x) = 0 \implies e^x(x+1) = 1$. At $x=0$, $1(1)=1$, matches.
   - $f(0) = \frac{2}{1+1} = 1$. Matches max value 1.
   - Check if it's a maximum: For $x<0$ (e.g., $x=-1$), $f'(-1) = \frac{1 - e^{-1}(0)}{(...)^2} > 0$. For $x>0$ (e.g., $x=1$), $f'(1) = \frac{1 - 2e}{(...)^2} < 0$. So yes, local max.
   - As $x \to \infty$, $f(x) \to 0$. As $x \to -\infty$, $f(x) \to -\infty$ (numerator $\to -\infty$, denominator $\to 1$). So global max is indeed 1 at $x=0$.
   - Matches option B.

7.  **Structure the Output:**
   - State the derivative calculation.
   - Set derivative to zero to find critical point condition.
   - Use the maximum value condition to set up equations.
   - Solve the system for $x_0$ and $a$.
   - Conclude $a=1$.
   - Match with option B.

**逐步推理过程：**

1. **确定数列 $\{a_i\}$ 的各项**
   根据题意：
   - $a_1 = 1$
   - $a_2 = a_3 = 3$
   - $a_4 = a_5 = 5$

2. **利用分组和的性质列方程**
   将这 12 个数分为 6 组，设每组两个数之和依次为 $S_1, S_2, \dots, S_6$。
   由题意，这 6 个和构成公差为 $d(d>0)$ 的等差数列。不妨设 $S_1 < S_2 < \dots < S_6$。
   所有组的和之和等于原数列总和，即：
   $$S_1 + S_2 + \dots + S_6 = 108$$
   利用等差数列求和公式：
   $$6S_1 + \frac{6 \times (6-1)}{2}d = 108 \Rightarrow 6S_1 + 15d = 108$$
   两边同时除以 3，得：
   $$2S_1 + 5d = 36 \quad \text{......(*)}$$

Prompt
请解答以下数学题（单选题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

8. 设U={(x_1, x_2, x_3)|x_i \in \{-2,-1,1,2"}, i=1,2,3}\}为空间中64个点构成的集合，点P(1,1,1)，记样本空间\Omega = C_U P，从\Omega中随机取一个点，定义随机变量X如下：对\Omega中的每个点A(x_1, x_2, x_3)，令X(A) = x_1 + x_2 + x_3，则X的数学期望值为（ ）
A. -\frac{1}{21}
B. -\frac{1}{63}
C. 0
D. \frac{1}{7}

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
**逐步推理过程：**

1. **明确集合与样本空间**
   集合 $U$ 由所有满足 $x_i \in \{-2, -1, 1, 2\} \ (i=1,2,3)$ 的点 $(x_1, x_2, x_3)$ 构成。每个坐标有 4 种可能，故 $|U| = 4^3 = 64$。
   符号 $C_U P$ 表示集合 $U$ 中除去单元素集合 $\{P\}$ 后的补集，即 $\Omega = U \setminus \{(1,1,1)\}$。因此样本空间 $\Omega$ 包含 $64 - 1 = 63$ 个点，从 $\Omega$ 中随机取一点，每点被抽到的概率均为 $\frac{1}{63}$。

2. **计算全集 $U$ 中随机变量 $X$ 的总和**
   设 $S_U = \sum_{A(x_1,x_2,x_3) \in U} X(A) = \sum_{A \in U} (x_1 + x_2 + x_3)$。
   利用求和的线性性质，可将其拆分为三个坐标分量的和：
   $$S_U = \sum_{A \in U} x_1 + \sum_{A \in U} x_2 + \sum_{A \in U} x_3$$
   以第一个分量为例：当 $x_1$ 固定时，$x_2$ 和 $x_3$ 各有 4 种独立取值，共 $4 \times 4 = 16$ 组。因此：
   $$\sum_{A \in U} x_1 = 16 \times \sum_{x_1 \in \{-2,-1,1,2\}} x_1$$
   由于坐标取值集合 $\{-2, -1, 1, 2\}$ 关于原点对称，其元素之和为 $(-2) + (-1) + 1 + 2 = 0$。
   故 $\sum_{A \in U} x_1 = 16 \times 0 = 0$。同理可得 $\sum_{A \in U} x_2 = 0$，$\sum_{A \in U} x_3 = 0$。
   从而 $S_U = 0 + 0 + 0 = 0$。即 $U$ 中所有点的坐标和之总和为 $0$。

3. **计算样本空间 $\Omega$ 中的总和**
   $\Omega$ 是 $U$ 去掉点 $P(1,1,1)$ 后的集合，因此 $\Omega$ 中所有点的 $X$ 值之和为：
   $$\sum_{A \in \Omega} X(A) = S_U - X(P) = 0 - (1 + 1 + 1) = -3$$

4. **求数学期望 $E[X]$**
   根据离散型随机变量期望的定义：
   $$E[X] = \frac{1}{|\Omega|} \sum_{A \in \Omega} X(A) = \frac{1}{63} \times (-3) = -\frac{3}{63} = -\frac{1}{21}$$

5. **对照选项**
   计算结果 $-\frac{1}{21}$ 与选项 A 完全一致。

\boxed{A}
Reasoning
Here's a thinking process:

1.  **Understand the User Input:**
   - **Problem Statement:** Let $U = \{(x_1, x_2, x_3) \mid x_i \in \{-2, -1, 1, 2\}, i=1,2,3\}$ be a set of 64 points in space. Point $P(1,1,1)$. Define the sample space $\Omega = C_U P$ (the set of all points in $U$ excluding $P$, or maybe the set of all points in $U$? Wait, $C_U P$ usually means the complement of $P$ in $U$, i.e., $U \setminus \{P\}$. Let's verify standard notation. In Chinese math problems, $C_U P$ or $C_U\{P\}$ usually means the complement of $\{P\}$ relative to $U$. So $\Omega = U \setminus \{P\}$. $|U| = 4^3 = 64$. So $|\Omega| = 63$.
   - **Random Variable:** For each $A(x_1, x_2, x_3) \in \Omega$, $X(A) = x_1 + x_2 + x_3$.
   - **Question:** Find the expected value $E[X]$.
   - **Options:** A. $-1/21$, B. $-1/63$, C. 0, D. $1/7$.

2.  **Clarify Notation & Definitions:**
   - $U$ contains all triples $(x_1, x_2, x_3)$ where each $x_i \in \{-2, -1, 1, 2\}$. Note that 0 is *not* in the set.
   - $|U| = 4 \times 4 \times 4 = 64$.
   - $P = (1, 1, 1)$.
   - $\Omega = C_U P$ means $\Omega = U \setminus \{(1,1,1)\}$. So $\Omega$ has 63 points.
   - We pick a point uniformly at random from $\Omega$. So each point in $\Omega$ has probability $1/63$.
   - $X(A) = x_1 + x_2 + x_3$.
   - We need $E[X] = \frac{1}{63} \sum_{(x_1,x_2,x_3) \in \Omega} (x_1 + x_2 + x_3)$.

3.  **Calculate the Sum over $U$:**
   - Let $S_U = \sum_{(x_1,x_2,x_3) \in U} (x_1 + x_2 + x_3)$.
   - By linearity of expectation/summation, $S_U = \sum_{(x_1,x_2,x_3) \in U} x_1 + \sum_{(x_1,x_2,x_3) \in U} x_2 + \sum_{(x_1,x_2,x_3) \in U} x_3$.
   - Consider $\sum_{(x_1,x_2,x_3) \in U} x_1$. For a fixed $x_1$, there are $4 \times 4 = 16$ choices for $(x_2, x_3)$. So the sum is $16 \times \sum_{x_1 \in \{-2,-1,1,2\}} x_1$.
   - $\sum_{x \in \{-2,-1,1,2\}} x = -2 - 1 + 1 + 2 = 0$.
   - Therefore, $\sum_{(x_1,x_2,x_3) \in U} x_1 = 16 \times 0 = 0$.
   - Similarly, $\sum_{(x_1,x_2,x_3) \in U} x_2 = 0$ and $\sum_{(x_1,x_2,x_3) \in U} x_3 = 0$.
   - So $S_U = 0$.

4.  **Calculate the Sum over $\Omega$:**
   - $\Omega = U \setminus \{P\}$, where $P = (1,1,1)$.
   - The sum over $\Omega$ is $S_\Omega = S_U - X(P)$.
   - $X(P) = 1 + 1 + 1 = 3$.
   - So $S_\Omega = 0 - 3 = -3$.

5.  **Calculate the Expected Value:**
   - $E[X] = \frac{S_\Omega}{|\Omega|} = \frac{-3}{63} = -\frac{1}{21}$.

6.  **Match with Options:**
   - Option A is $-1/21$.
   - This matches perfectly.

7.  **Double Check Everything:**
   - Set $U$: $x_i \in \{-2, -1, 1, 2\}$. Sum of elements is 0. Correct.
   - Total points: $4^3 = 64$. Correct.

   - Expected value formula: $\frac{1}{63} \sum_{A \in \Omega} (x_1+x_2+x_3)$. Correct.
   - Sum over $U$: Each coordinate sum is 0 because the set is symmetric around 0 and has equal number of positive and negative values? Wait, $\{-2, -1, 1, 2\}$ sums to 0. Yes.
   - So $\sum_{U} x_1 = 4^2 \times 0 = 0$. Same for $x_2, x_3$. Total sum over $U$ is 0.
   - Remove $P(1,1,1)$, sum becomes $0 - 3 = -3$.
   - Divide by 63: $-3/63 = -1/21$.
   - Options: A. $-1/21$, B. $-1/63$, C. 0, D. $1/7$.
   - Answer is A.

Prompt
请解答以下数学题（多选题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

9. 设z = 3 + 2i，则（ ）
A. \bar{z} = 3 - 2i
B. |z| = 5
C. z^2 = 5 + 12i
D. \frac{z+3}{z-i} \in \mathbf{R}

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
已知复数 $z = 3 + 2i$，我们逐项进行验证：

**A. $\bar{z} = 3 - 2i$**
复数的共轭复数是将虚部变号。对于 $z = a + bi$，其共轭复数为 $\bar{z} = a - bi$。
此处 $a=3, b=2$，故 $\bar{z} = 3 - 2i$。
$\Rightarrow$ **A正确**。

**B. $|z| = 5$**
复数的模长计算公式为 $|z| = \sqrt{a^2 + b^2}$。
代入得 $|z| = \sqrt{3^2 + 2^2} = \sqrt{9 + 4} = \sqrt{13}$。
因为 $\sqrt{13} \neq 5$，
$\Rightarrow$ **B错误**。

**C. $z^2 = 5 + 12i$**
直接计算平方：
$z^2 = (3 + 2i)^2 = 3^2 + 2 \cdot 3 \cdot 2i + (2i)^2 = 9 + 12i + 4i^2$
由于 $i^2 = -1$，所以 $z^2 = 9 + 12i - 4 = 5 + 12i$。
$\Rightarrow$ **C正确**。

**D. $\frac{z+3}{z-i} \in \mathbf{R}$**
先分别计算分子和分母：
$z + 3 = (3 + 2i) + 3 = 6 + 2i$
$z - i = (3 + 2i) - i = 3 + i$
代入分式得：
$\frac{z+3}{z-i} = \frac{6 + 2i}{3 + i} = \frac{2(3 + i)}{3 + i} = 2$
因为 $2$ 是实数，即结果属于 $\mathbf{R}$。
$\Rightarrow$ **D正确**。

综上所述，正确的选项为 A、C、D。

最终答案：$\boxed{ACD}$
Reasoning

1.  **Analyze User Input:**
   - **Question:** Multiple choice question about complex numbers.

2.  **Evaluate Each Option:**
   - **Given:** $z = 3 + 2i$
   - **Option A:** $\bar{z} = 3 - 2i$
     - Definition: The complex conjugate of $a + bi$ is $a - bi$.
     - Here, $a=3, b=2$, so $\bar{z} = 3 - 2i$.
     - Option A is **True**.
   - **Option B:** $|z| = 5$
     - Definition: $|z| = \sqrt{a^2 + b^2}$.
     - Here, $|z| = \sqrt{3^2 + 2^2} = \sqrt{9 + 4} = \sqrt{13}$.
     - $\sqrt{13} \neq 5$.
     - Option B is **False**.

     - Since $i^2 = -1$, $z^2 = 9 + 12i - 4 = 5 + 12i$.
     - Option C is **True**.
   - **Option D:** $\frac{z+3}{z-i} \in \mathbf{R}$
     - Calculate numerator: $z + 3 = (3 + 2i) + 3 = 6 + 2i$.
     - Calculate denominator: $z - i = (3 + 2i) - i = 3 + i$.
     - So the expression is $\frac{6 + 2i}{3 + i}$.
     - Simplify by multiplying numerator and denominator by the conjugate of the denominator ($3 - i$):
       $$\frac{(6 + 2i)(3 - i)}{(3 + i)(3 - i)} = \frac{18 - 6i + 6i - 2i^2}{9 - i^2} = \frac{18 - 2(-1)}{9 - (-1)} = \frac{18 + 2}{10} = \frac{20}{10} = 2.$$
     - Since 2 is a real number, the expression is in $\mathbf{R}$.
     - Option D is **True**.

gaokao_000_009 ✓ BC BC 36175 105.8 341.9 http://192.168.0.41:9292
Prompt
请解答以下数学题（多选题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

10. 在空间中，A、B为两个定点，动点C到直线AB的距离为2，动点D到直线AB的距离为1。若二面角C - AB - D为60°，则（ ）
A. \angle CAD \ge 60^\circ
B. CD \ge \sqrt{3}
C. 当AB \perp CD时，CD \perp平面ABD
D. 当AB \perp平面ACD时，AC \perp AD

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
**解题思路与逐步推理**

**1. 建立空间直角坐标系**
根据题意，我们将直线 $AB$ 设为 $z$ 轴，定点 $A$ 设为原点 $(0,0,0)$。
*   动点 $C$ 到直线 $AB$ 的距离为 2，且二面角 $C-AB-D$ 为 $60^\circ$。设 $C$ 在平面 $ABC$ 内，坐标可表示为 $C(2, 0, z_C)$。
*   动点 $D$ 到直线 $AB$ 的距离为 1。由于二面角为 $60^\circ$，$D$ 在平面 $ABD$ 内，坐标可表示为 $D(\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, z_D)$。

**2. 逐项分析**

*   **选项 A：$\angle CAD \ge 60^\circ$**
    向量 $\vec{AC} = (2, 0, z_C)$，$\vec{AD} = (\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, z_D)$。
    $\cos \angle CAD = \frac{\vec{AC} \cdot \vec{AD}}{|\vec{AC}| \cdot |\vec{AD}|} = \frac{1 + z_C z_D}{\sqrt{4 + z_C^2}\sqrt{1 + z_D^2}}$。
    当 $z_C, z_D$ 趋于无穷大时，分子分母比值趋于 1，即 $\cos \angle CAD \to 1$，$\angle CAD \to 0^\circ$。因此，$\angle CAD$ 不一定大于等于 $60^\circ$。**A 错误**。

*   **选项 B：$CD \ge \sqrt{3}$**
    $CD$ 的平方为：$CD^2 = (2 - \frac{1}{2})^2 + (0 - \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2})^2 + (z_C - z_D)^2 = \frac{9}{4} + \frac{3}{4} + (z_C - z_D)^2 = 3 + (z_C - z_D)^2$。
    因为 $(z_C - z_D)^2 \ge 0$，所以 $CD^2 \ge 3$，即 $CD \ge \sqrt{3}$。**B 正确**。

*   **选项 C：当 $AB \perp CD$ 时，$CD \perp$ 平面 $ABD$**
    $AB \perp CD \implies \vec{AB} \cdot \vec{CD} = 0 \implies z_C = z_D$。
    此时 $C, D$ 在同一高度，$\vec{CD} = (-\frac{3}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, 0)$。
    平面 $ABD$ 包含 $z$ 轴（$AB$）和点 $D$。其法向量 $\vec{n}$ 在水平面内且垂直于 $D$ 点的位置向量 $\vec{AD}$（方向为 $60^\circ$），即 $\vec{n}$ 的方向为 $150^\circ$。
    观察向量 $\vec{CD}$，其斜率为 $\frac{\sqrt{3}/2}{-3/2} = -\frac{\sqrt{3}}{3}$，对应 $150^\circ$。
    故 $\vec{CD}$ 平行于平面 $ABD$ 的法向量，即 $CD \perp$ 平面 $ABD$。**C 正确**。

*   **选项 D：当 $AB \perp$ 平面 $ACD$ 时，$AC \perp AD$**
    $AB \perp$ 平面 $ACD \implies AB \perp AC$ 且 $AB \perp AD$。这意味着 $C, D$ 均在与 $AB$ 垂直的过 $A$ 点的平面上，此时 $z_C = z_D = 0$。
    在该平面内，$\angle CAD$ 等于二面角 $C-AB-D$ 的大小，即 $\angle CAD = 60^\circ$。
    因此，$AC$ 与 $AD$ 不垂直。**D 错误**。

**最终结论**
正确的选项为 B 和 C。

\boxed{BC}
Reasoning
Here's a thinking process to solve the given geometry problem:

1.  **Understand the Setup:**
    *   **Fixed Points:** $A, B$ are fixed points in space. Let the line $AB$ be the $z$-axis (or a reference line). Let $A$ be the origin $(0,0,0)$ for simplicity, or just a point on the line. The line direction is fixed.
    *   **Moving Point $C$:** The distance from $C$ to the line $AB$ is 2. This means $C$ lies on a cylinder of radius 2 centered on the line $AB$. Let's project $C$ onto the line $AB$ at point $C'$. Then $CC' = 2$ and $CC' \perp AB$.
    *   **Moving Point $D$:** The distance from $D$ to the line $AB$ is 1. This means $D$ lies on a cylinder of radius 1 centered on the line $AB$. Let's project $D$ onto the line $AB$ at point $D'$. Then $DD' = 1$ and $DD' \perp AB$.
    *   **Dihedral Angle:** The dihedral angle $C - AB - D$ is $60^\circ$. The planes involved are plane $ABC$ and plane $ABD$. Since $CC' \perp AB$ and $DD' \perp AB$, the angle between the vector $\vec{C'C}$ (or $\vec{C'C}$ shifted) and $\vec{D'D}$ (or $\vec{D'D}$ shifted) relates to this angle. Actually, strictly speaking, the dihedral angle is the angle between the two planes intersecting at $AB$. Let's visualize the cross-section perpendicular to $AB$.
    *   **Coordinate System:**
        *   Let the line $AB$ be the $z$-axis.
        *   Let $A$ be at the origin $(0,0,0)$.
        *   Let $B$ be at $(0,0, h)$ where $h$ is the distance $AB$. Or just keep $B$ generic on $z$-axis.
        *   Let the projection of $C$ onto $AB$ be $P_C$. Let $P_C = (0, 0, z_C)$.
        *   Since distance $C$ to line $AB$ is 2, $C$ can be represented as $(2 \cos \theta_C, 2 \sin \theta_C, z_C)$.
        *   Let the projection of $D$ onto $AB$ be $P_D$. Let $P_D = (0, 0, z_D)$.
        *   Since distance $D$ to line $AB$ is 1, $D$ can be represented as $(1 \cos \theta_D, 1 \sin \theta_D, z_D)$.
        *   **Dihedral Angle Condition:** The dihedral angle is the angle between the plane containing $A, B, C$ and the plane containing $A, B, D$.
            *   Plane $ABC$ contains the $z$-axis and $C$. The normal to this plane is perpendicular to $z$-axis and $\vec{P_C C}$. Wait, the plane passes through the $z$-axis. So the equation of the plane is determined by the angle of $C$'s projection.
            *   Actually, the dihedral angle $C - AB - D$ is the angle between the plane $(ABC)$ and $(ABD)$. Since $A, B$ are on the $z$-axis, these planes intersect at the $z$-axis. The angle is simply the difference in the azimuthal angles of $C$ and $D$.
            *   Let's verify this. Let $P_C$ be the projection of $C$ on $AB$. Then $CP_C \perp AB$. Let $P_D$ be projection of $D$ on $AB$. Then $DP_D \perp AB$. The angle between planes $(ABC)$ and $(ABD)$ is the angle between the lines $CP_C$ and $DP_D$ *if* $P_C$ and $P_D$ are the same point? No.
            *   Definition: The angle between two intersecting planes is the angle between two lines, one in each plane, both perpendicular to the line of intersection at the same point.
            *   So, we can look at a cross-section perpendicular to $AB$ at some point. Let's pick an arbitrary point $O$ on $AB$.
            *   Project $C$ to $C'$ on plane perpendicular to $AB$ passing through some point. Actually, simpler: The direction of vector $\vec{AC}$ projected onto $xy$-plane makes an angle $\theta_C$. The direction of vector $\vec{AD}$ projected onto $xy$-plane makes an angle $\theta_D$.
            *   The dihedral angle is $|\theta_C - \theta_D| = 60^\circ$ (or $\pi/3$). Let's assume without loss of generality that $C$ is at angle $0$ and $D$ is at angle $60^\circ$ (or $-60^\circ$, doesn't matter due to symmetry).
            *   Let's set coordinates:
                *   Line $AB$ is the $z$-axis.
                *   $A = (0,0,0)$.
                *   $B$ is on $z$-axis, say $(0,0,b)$. $b$ can be anything, but distance $AB$ is fixed? "A, B are two fixed points". Yes.
                *   Wait, the problem doesn't specify distance $AB$. Let's call it $d_{AB}$.
                *   $C$ projects to $P_C$ on $AB$. $C$ is at distance 2 from $AB$. So $C = (2, 0, z_C)$. (Rotated coordinates so $C$ is on x-axis).

    Let's analyze each option.

    **Coordinates recap:**
    $A = (0,0,0)$.
    Line $AB$ is the $z$-axis.
    $C$ is on plane $y=0$ (WLOG), distance 2 from $z$-axis.
    So $C = (2, 0, z_C)$.
    $D$ is on plane passing through $z$-axis with angle $60^\circ$ to $xz$-plane.
    So $D = (1 \cos 60^\circ, 1 \sin 60^\circ, z_D) = (\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, z_D)$.

    Wait, does the dihedral angle definition restrict $z$ coordinates?
    Dihedral angle $C-AB-D$.
    Plane $ABC$ contains $A(0,0,0)$, $B(0,0,L)$, $C(2,0,z_C)$.
    Since $A, B$ are on $z$-axis, plane $ABC$ is the plane containing the $z$-axis and $C$. Since $C=(2,0,z_C)$, this is the plane $y=0$.
    Plane $ABD$ contains $A(0,0,0)$, $B(0,0,L)$, $D(\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, z_D)$.
    This is a plane containing the $z$-axis and $D$.
    The angle between plane $y=0$ and plane containing $(0,0,0)$, $(0,0,1)$, $(\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, 0)$ is $60^\circ$.
    The normal to $y=0$ is $(0,1,0)$.
    The normal to plane $ABD$ can be found. Vectors in plane are $(0,0,1)$ and $(\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, z_D)$.
    Cross product: $(0,0,1) \times (\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, z_D) = (-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, \frac{1}{2}, 0)$.
    Normal vector $\vec{n}_{ABD} = (-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, \frac{1}{2}, 0)$. (Or proportional to $(-\sqrt{3}, 1, 0)$).
    Normal to $ABC$ is $\vec{n}_{ABC} = (0,1,0)$.
    Cosine of angle between normals: $\frac{(0,1,0) \cdot (-\sqrt{3}, 1, 0)}{1 \cdot 2} = \frac{1}{2}$.
    So angle is $60^\circ$. Correct.
    Note that $z_C$ and $z_D$ do not affect the dihedral angle. They only determine the "height" of $C$ and $D$ along the line $AB$.

    **Analyze Option A: $\angle CAD \ge 60^\circ$**
    Vector $\vec{AC} = C - A = (2, 0, z_C)$.
    Vector $\vec{AD} = D - A = (\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, z_D)$.
    Cosine of angle $\theta = \angle CAD$:
    $\cos \theta = \frac{\vec{AC} \cdot \vec{AD}}{|\vec{AC}| |\vec{AD}|}$
    Numerator: $2(\frac{1}{2}) + 0(\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}) + z_C z_D = 1 + z_C z_D$.
    Denominator: $|\vec{AC}| = \sqrt{4 + z_C^2}$.
    $|\vec{AD}| = \sqrt{\frac{1}{4} + \frac{3}{4} + z_D^2} = \sqrt{1 + z_D^2}$.
    So $\cos \theta = \frac{1 + z_C z_D}{\sqrt{4 + z_C^2} \sqrt{1 + z_D^2}}$.
    We want to check if $\theta \ge 60^\circ$, which means $\cos \theta \le \cos 60^\circ = \frac{1}{2}$.
    Is $\frac{1 + z_C z_D}{\sqrt{4 + z_C^2} \sqrt{1 + z_D^2}} \le \frac{1}{2}$ always true?
    Let's test some values.
    Case 1: $z_C = 0, z_D = 0$.
    $C = (2,0,0)$, $D = (1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    $\vec{AC} = (2,0,0)$, $\vec{AD} = (1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    Dot product = 1. Lengths = 2, 1.
    $\cos \theta = 1 / (2 \cdot 1) = 1/2$.
    $\theta = 60^\circ$.
    So $\theta \ge 60^\circ$ holds (equality).
    Case 2: $z_C$ large, $z_D$ large positive.
    Let $z_C = k, z_D = k$ for large $k$.
    $\cos \theta \approx \frac{k^2}{\sqrt{k^2}\sqrt{k^2}} = 1$.
    So $\theta \approx 0^\circ$.
    Wait, if $\theta \to 0$, then $\theta \ge 60^\circ$ is **false**.
    Let's check calculation.
    $z_C = 10, z_D = 10$.
    Num: $1 + 100 = 101$.
    Denom: $\sqrt{4+100} \sqrt{1+100} = \sqrt{104} \sqrt{101} \approx 10.2 \cdot 10.05 \approx 102.5$.
    $\cos \theta \approx 101/102.5 < 1$.
    Wait, $101/102.5 \approx 0.985$.
    $\cos 60^\circ = 0.5$.
    $0.985 > 0.5$, so $\theta < 60^\circ$.
    So option A is **False**.

    Let me double check the problem statement.
    "A, B fixed points".
    Maybe $z_C$ and $z_D$ are constrained?
    "动点C到直线AB的距离为2" - $C$ is on cylinder.
    "动点D到直线AB的距离为1" - $D$ is on cylinder.
    "二面角C-AB-D为60" - angle between planes is 60.
    This does not constrain $z_C, z_D$ relative to each other, other than the angle.
    However, if $A$ is origin, $C$ and $D$ are vectors from origin.
    Wait, if $C$ and $D$ are very far away, the angle $\angle CAD$ is determined by the angle between the vectors $\vec{AC}$ and $\vec{AD}$.
    As $z \to \infty$, the vectors become almost parallel to the $z$-axis. The angle between them goes to 0.
    The angle between their projections on $xy$-plane is $60^\circ$.
    But the $z$-component dominates.
    So $\angle CAD$ can be arbitrarily small.
    Thus A is definitely false.

    Wait, maybe I misinterpreted "二面角".
    Dihedral angle is fixed at 60.
    Is it possible that $C$ and $D$ are constrained to be on the *same* side of something? No.
    Is it possible that the question implies $C, D$ are on specific lines? No, "moving points".
    Let's re-read carefully.
    Maybe I set up coordinates wrong?
    Distance to line $AB$ is fixed.
    Dihedral angle is angle between planes.
    Yes, setup is correct.
    Why would A be an option? Maybe it's true for some specific condition? No, "则" (then) implies for *all* such $C, D$.
    So A is False.

    Let's check B.
    **Option B: $CD \ge \sqrt{3}$**
    $C = (2, 0, z_C)$.
    $D = (\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, z_D)$.
    Vector $\vec{CD} = D - C = (\frac{1}{2} - 2, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} - 0, z_D - z_C) = (-\frac{3}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, z_D - z_C)$.
    Distance squared $CD^2 = (-\frac{3}{2})^2 + (\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2})^2 + (z_D - z_C)^2$.
    $CD^2 = \frac{9}{4} + \frac{3}{4} + (z_D - z_C)^2 = \frac{12}{4} + (z_D - z_C)^2 = 3 + (z_D - z_C)^2$.
    Since $(z_D - z_C)^2 \ge 0$, $CD^2 \ge 3$.
    So $CD \ge \sqrt{3}$.
    Minimum value is $\sqrt{3}$ when $z_C = z_D$.
    Is it possible for $z_C = z_D$?
    $C$ is at distance 2 from line, $D$ is at distance 1 from line.
    They are on cylinders.
    $z_C$ and $z_D$ are independent coordinates (heights).
    We can choose $C$ and $D$ at the same height (e.g., $z=0$).
    Then $C=(2,0,0)$, $D=(1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    Distance is $\sqrt{(2-0.5)^2 + (0-\sqrt{3}/2)^2 + 0} = \sqrt{1.5^2 + 0.75} = \sqrt{2.25 + 0.75} = \sqrt{3}$.
    So minimum is $\sqrt{3}$.
    So **Option B is True**.

    Let's check C.
    **Option C: 当 $AB \perp CD$ 时， $CD \perp$ 平面 $ABD$**
    Condition: $AB \perp CD$.
    Line $AB$ is along $z$-axis, direction vector $\vec{k} = (0,0,1)$.
    Vector $\vec{CD} = (-\frac{3}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, z_D - z_C)$.
    Dot product $\vec{AB} \cdot \vec{CD} = 0 \cdot (-\frac{3}{2}) + 0 \cdot (\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}) + 1 \cdot (z_D - z_C) = z_D - z_C$.
    So $AB \perp CD$ implies $z_D - z_C = 0$, i.e., $z_C = z_D$.
    So $C$ and $D$ are at the same height.
    In this case, $\vec{CD} = (-\frac{3}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, 0)$.
    We need to check if $\vec{CD} \perp$ Plane $ABD$.
    Plane $ABD$ contains the $z$-axis and the point $D(\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, 0)$ (since $z_D=z_C$).
    Wait, plane $ABD$ contains vector $\vec{AD}$ and $\vec{AB}$ (z-axis).
    Actually, if $z_C = z_D$, let's call it $z_0$.
    $D = (\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, z_0)$.
    Vector $\vec{AD} = (\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, z_0)$.
    Vector $\vec{AB} = (0,0,1)$.
    Normal to plane $ABD$ is $\vec{n} = \vec{AB} \times \vec{AD}$ (or cross product of any two vectors in plane).
    $\vec{n} = (0,0,1) \times (\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, z_0) = (-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, \frac{1}{2}, 0)$.
    (Calculated earlier).
    Is $\vec{CD}$ parallel to $\vec{n}$?
    $\vec{CD} = (-\frac{3}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, 0)$.
    $\vec{n} = (-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, \frac{1}{2}, 0)$.
    Are they parallel?
    Ratio of x-components: $\frac{-3/2}{-\sqrt{3}/2} = \frac{3}{\sqrt{3}} = \sqrt{3}$.
    Ratio of y-components: $\frac{\sqrt{3}/2}{1/2} = \sqrt{3}$.
    Yes, $\vec{CD} = \sqrt{3} \vec{n}$.
    So $\vec{CD}$ is parallel to the normal of plane $ABD$.
    Therefore, $CD \perp$ Plane $ABD$.
    So **Option C is True**.

    Let's check D.
    **Option D: 当 $AB \perp$ 平面 $ACD$ 时， $AC \perp AD$**
    Condition: $AB \perp$ Plane $ACD$.
    Line $AB$ is $z$-axis.
    Plane $ACD$ contains $A(0,0,0)$, $C(2, 0, z_C)$, $D(\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, z_D)$.
    If $AB \perp$ Plane $ACD$, then $AB$ is normal to the plane.
    This means the normal vector of plane $ACD$ is parallel to $z$-axis.
    Or, simply, the plane $ACD$ must be perpendicular to the $z$-axis.
    Since $A$ is on the $z$-axis, the plane $ACD$ would be the plane $z=0$ (or parallel to it? No, passes through A).
    Wait, if a plane passes through origin and is perpendicular to $z$-axis, its equation is $z=0$.
    This implies that all points in the plane must have $z=0$.
    So $C$ must have $z_C = 0$ and $D$ must have $z_D = 0$.
    Let's check if this is forced.
    Plane $ACD$ contains vectors $\vec{AC} = (2, 0, z_C)$ and $\vec{AD} = (\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, z_D)$.
    Normal $\vec{n}_{ACD} = \vec{AC} \times \vec{AD}$.
    $\vec{n}_{ACD} = \det \begin{pmatrix} \mathbf{i} & \mathbf{j} & \mathbf{k} \\ 2 & 0 & z_C \\ 1/2 & \sqrt{3}/2 & z_D \end{pmatrix}$
    $= \mathbf{i}(0 \cdot z_D - z_C \cdot \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}) - \mathbf{j}(2 \cdot z_D - z_C \cdot \frac{1}{2}) + \mathbf{k}(2 \cdot \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} - 0 \cdot \frac{1}{2})$
    $= (-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} z_C, \frac{1}{2} z_C - 2 z_D, \sqrt{3})$.
    For $AB$ (direction $(0,0,1)$) to be perpendicular to plane $ACD$, $\vec{n}_{ACD}$ must be parallel to $(0,0,1)$.
    So the $x$ and $y$ components must be 0.
    $x$-comp: $-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} z_C = 0 \implies z_C = 0$.
    $y$-comp: $\frac{1}{2} z_C - 2 z_D = 0$. Since $z_C=0$, this implies $-2z_D = 0 \implies z_D = 0$.
    So the condition $AB \perp$ Plane $ACD$ implies $z_C = 0$ and $z_D = 0$.
    So $C = (2, 0, 0)$ and $D = (\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, 0)$.
    Now we check if $AC \perp AD$.
    $\vec{AC} = (2, 0, 0)$.
    $\vec{AD} = (\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, 0)$.
    Dot product $\vec{AC} \cdot \vec{AD} = 2(\frac{1}{2}) + 0 + 0 = 1$.
    Since dot product is 1 (non-zero), the vectors are **not perpendicular**.
    So **Option D is False**.

    Let me re-evaluate D.
    Maybe I misinterpreted "AB \perp 平面 ACD".
    $AB$ is a line. $ACD$ is a plane.
    Line perpendicular to plane means line is orthogonal to every line in the plane passing through intersection.
    Intersection is A.
    So $AB \perp AC$ and $AB \perp AD$.

    $= 9/4 + 3/4 + \Delta z^2 = 3 + \Delta z^2 \ge 3$.

    Check C: If $AB \perp CD$, then $CD \perp$ Plane $ABD$.

    However, usually in geometry problems involving dihedral angles, we consider the geometry defined.

    Let $r_C = 2, r_D = 1$.
    Distance $CD^2 = r_C^2 + r_D^2 - 2 r_C r_D \cos \alpha + (z_C - z_D)^2$.
    $CD^2 = 4 + 1 - 4 \cos \alpha + \Delta z^2 = 5 - 4 \cos \alpha + \Delta z^2$.
    If $\alpha = 60^\circ$, $\cos 60 = 1/2$. $CD^2 = 5 - 2 + \Delta z^2 = 3 + \Delta z^2 \ge 3$.
    If $\alpha = 120^\circ$, $\cos 120 = -1/2$. $CD^2 = 5 + 2 + \Delta z^2 = 7 + \Delta z^2 \ge 7$.
    In both cases, $CD \ge \sqrt{3}$.
    So Option B is robustly True.

    So $CD \perp$ Plane $ABD$.
    True.

    **Double Check Option D again.**
    If $AB \perp$ Plane $ACD$.
    Then $AB \perp AC$ and $AB \perp AD$.
    So $C, D$ in plane $\perp AB$ at $A$.
    Angle between planes $ABC$ and $ABD$ is angle between $AC$ and $AD$.
    Given as 60.
    So $\angle CAD = 60$.
    Option says $AC \perp AD$ (90).
    False.

    **Are there any other interpretations?**
    Maybe $C$ and $D$ are on the *same* side of $AB$ in terms of projection?
    Dihedral angle 60 is small.
    If $C$ and $D$ were far apart vertically, distance $CD$ would be large.
    But we are looking for bounds.
    Minimum distance is $\sqrt{3}$.
    Is it possible to have $CD < \sqrt{3}$?
    Only if angle between radial vectors is less than 60.
    But dihedral angle is fixed.
    Wait, "Dihedral angle C-AB-D" is 60.
    Does this restrict the *relative* position of $C$ and $D$ to be 60?
    Yes, the angle between the planes defined by $C$ and $D$ is 60.
    The angle between the planes is determined by the azimuthal angle difference.
    So $|\theta_C - \theta_D| = 60$ (or 120, but usually 60).
    Even if 120, distance is larger.
    So min distance is $\sqrt{3}$.

    **What if A, B are not origin/axis?**
    Just a translation/rotation. Distances and angles are invariant.
    So analysis holds.

    **Let's check the options again.**
    A. $\angle CAD \ge 60^\circ$.
    Counter-example: $C(2, 0, 100)$, $D(0.5, 0.866, 100)$.

    **Wait, is it possible to interpret "二面角 C-AB-D" differently?**

    **Is there any trick with "A, B fixed points"?**
    Maybe $A$ is not on the line perpendicular to $C$'s projection?
    Distance to line $AB$ is distance to infinite line.
    Yes.
    $C$ projects to $P_C$ on line $AB$.
    $CP_C = 2$.

    $DP_D = 1$.
    $P_C$ and $P_D$ are just points on the line.

    My analysis holds.

    **Let's re-read Option C carefully.**
    "当 $AB \perp CD$ 时， $CD \perp$ 平面 $ABD$".
    We found that if $AB \perp CD$, then $z_C = z_D$ (assuming $AB$ vertical).
    And then $CD \perp$ Plane $ABD$ held.
    Is there any geometric reason why this might fail?
    Maybe if $C, D, A, B$ are coplanar?
    If $C, D, A, B$ coplanar, then $D$ must be in plane $ABC$.
    But dihedral angle is 60. So not coplanar.
    So planes are distinct.
    Intersection is line $AB$.
    $CD$ connects a point in plane 1 to a point in plane 2.
    $AB \perp CD$ means $CD$ is orthogonal to the intersection line.
    In a dihedral angle configuration, if you take a segment $CD$ perpendicular to the edge $AB$, is it perpendicular to the other plane?
    Let edge be $L$. $C \in \Pi_1, D \in \Pi_2$.
    $CD \perp L$.
    Does $CD \perp \Pi_2$?
    For $CD$ to be perp to $\Pi_2$, $CD$ must be perp to every line in $\Pi_2$.
    Specifically, $CD \perp AD$ (if $A$ on $L$) and $CD \perp AB$.
    We know $CD \perp AB$ (given).
    Is $CD \perp AD$?
    $AD$ is in $\Pi_2$.
    In our coordinate calculation:
    $C = (2, 0, 0)$, $D = (1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$ (since $z_C=z_D=0$).
    $A = (0,0,0)$.

    $\vec{AD} = (0.5, 0.866, 0)$.
    Dot product: $-0.75 + 0.75 = 0$.
    Yes, $CD \perp AD$.
    Since $CD \perp AB$ and $CD \perp AD$, $CD$ is perpendicular to two intersecting lines in plane $ABD$.
    So $CD \perp$ Plane $ABD$.
    This seems generally true if $CD \perp AB$ and the geometry is specific?
    Wait, let's check generally.
    Let $A$ be origin. $AB$ along $z$.
    $C$ in plane $\theta=0$. $D$ in plane $\theta=60$.
    $C = (2, 0, z_C)$. $D = (1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, z_D)$.
    $CD \perp AB \implies z_C = z_D$. Let $z=0$.
    $C=(2,0,0)$, $D=(1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    $\vec{AD} = (1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    $\vec{CD} = (-3/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    $\vec{AD} \cdot \vec{CD} = -3/4 + 3/4 = 0$.
    Yes.
    Is this a coincidence?
    $\vec{AC} = (2, 0, 0)$. $\vec{AD} = (1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    $\vec{CD} = \vec{AD} - \vec{AC}$.
    $\vec{CD} \cdot \vec{AD} = (\vec{AD} - \vec{AC}) \cdot \vec{AD} = |\vec{AD}|^2 - \vec{AC} \cdot \vec{AD}$.
    $|\vec{AD}| = 1$ (since dist to axis is 1 and $z=0$).
    $\vec{AC} \cdot \vec{AD} = |\vec{AC}| |\vec{AD}| \cos 60 = 2 \cdot 1 \cdot (1/2) = 1$.
    So dot product $= 1 - 1 = 0$.
    So yes, $CD \perp AD$ always holds when $z_C=z_D$ (i.e. $CD \perp AB$) given the distances 2 and 1 and angle 60.
    Why? Because $|\vec{AD}| = 1$ and $|\vec{AC}| = 2$ and angle is 60.
    It's a property of the triangle $ACD$ in the cross section?
    Triangle sides: $AC=2, AD=1, \angle CAD = 60$.
    By Law of Cosines: $CD^2 = 2^2 + 1^2 - 2(2)(1)\cos 60 = 4 + 1 - 2 = 3$.
    So $CD = \sqrt{3}$.
    Notice $1^2 + (\sqrt{3})^2 = 1 + 3 = 4 = 2^2$.
    So $AD^2 + CD^2 = AC^2$.
    So triangle $ADC$ is right-angled at $D$.
    So $CD \perp AD$.
    Since $CD \perp AB$ (given) and $CD \perp AD$ (derived), $CD$ is perpendicular to plane $ABD$.
    So Option C is mathematically solid.

    **Let's check Option D again with this triangle insight.**

    "Dihedral angle C-AB-D is 60".
    Standard convention: The angle between half-planes $ABC$ and $ABD$.

    Ray from axis to $C$.

    Let's check if there are any edge cases.
    $A, B$ fixed. $C, D$ moving.

    Wait, is there any interpretation where $A$ is not on the line segment $AB$?

    $AC \cdot AD = 2 \cdot 1 \cdot \cos 60 = 1 \ne 0$.
    Consistent.

    What if $A$ is not involved in the "distance to line" definition?
    Distance to line is shortest distance.

    Let's double check the calculation for B.
    $CD^2 = (x_C - x_D)^2 + (y_C - y_D)^2 + (z_C - z_D)^2$.
    $x_C^2 + y_C^2 = 2^2 = 4$.
    $x_D^2 + y_D^2 = 1^2 = 1$.
    Angle between $(x_C, y_C)$ and $(x_D, y_D)$ is 60.
    $(x_C, y_C) \cdot (x_D, y_D) = 2 \cdot 1 \cdot \cos 60 = 1$.
    $|\vec{r_C} - \vec{r_D}|^2 = |\vec{r_C}|^2 + |\vec{r_D}|^2 - 2 \vec{r_C} \cdot \vec{r_D} = 4 + 1 - 2(1) = 3$.
    So horizontal distance squared is 3.
    Total distance squared is $3 + (z_C - z_D)^2$.
    $\ge 3$.
    So $CD \ge \sqrt{3}$.
    Correct.

    What if angle was 120?
    Dot product $2 \cdot 1 \cdot \cos 120 = -1$.
    $|\vec{r_C} - \vec{r_D}|^2 = 4 + 1 - 2(-1) = 7$.
    Distance $\ge \sqrt{7}$.
    So B holds regardless.

    Let's double check C with vectors.
    $AB$ direction $\vec{k}$.
    $\vec{CD} \cdot \vec{k} = 0 \implies z_C = z_D$.
    $\vec{CD} = \vec{r_D} - \vec{r_C}$.
    Plane $ABD$ is spanned by $\vec{k}$ and $\vec{r_D}$ (since $A$ is origin, $D$ is $\vec{r_D} + z\vec{k}$).
    Wait, $D$ is $(\vec{r_D}, z_D)$.
    Plane $ABD$ contains origin, $z$-axis, and $D$.
    So it contains $\vec{k}$ and $\vec{OD} = (\vec{r_D}, 0)$.
    Normal $\vec{n} = \vec{k} \times \vec{OD} = (0,0,1) \times (x_D, y_D, 0) = (-y_D, x_D, 0)$.
    We need to check if $\vec{CD}$ is parallel to $\vec{n}$.
    $\vec{CD} = (x_D - x_C, y_D - y_C, 0)$.
    Parallel if $(x_D - x_C, y_D - y_C) = \lambda (-y_D, x_D)$.
    Or cross product in 2D is 0: $(x_D - x_C)x_D - (y_D - y_C)(-y_D) = 0$? No.
    Parallel means $(x_D - x_C)/(-y_D) = (y_D - y_C)/x_D$.
    $x_D(x_D - x_C) + y_D(y_D - y_C) = 0$.
    $x_D^2 - x_D x_C + y_D^2 - y_D y_C = 0$.
    $x_D^2 + y_D^2 - (x_D x_C + y_D y_C) = 0$.
    $|\vec{r_D}|^2 - \vec{r_D} \cdot \vec{r_C} = 0$.

    But it is 60.
    So no.

    So B and C.

    Wait, let me look at Option C again.
    "When $AB \perp CD$, then $CD \perp$ Plane $ABD$".
    This is a conditional statement.
    If the condition $AB \perp CD$ is met, the conclusion must hold.
    We found that $AB \perp CD \implies z_C = z_D$.
    And in that case ($z_C=z_D$), $CD \perp$ Plane $ABD$ is true.
    So the implication is true.
    Is it possible that condition is never met?
    No, we can choose $z_C = z_D$.
    So it's a valid implication.

    What if $C$ and $D$ are such that $AB \perp CD$ but $CD$ is not perpendicular to plane?
    We proved that if $AB \perp CD$, then $CD \perp$ plane.
    So no counter example.

    So B and C.

    Let's check if there is any subtle point about "fixed points A, B".
    Maybe $A$ is not the projection of $C$'s plane?
    Wait, plane $ACD$ passes through $A$.
    Plane $ABD$ passes through $A, B$.
    If $A$ is far from the "bulk" of $C, D$?
    Doesn't change angles or distances relative to line.

    Wait, could Option A be true?
    Maybe $\angle CAD$ is angle between vectors $\vec{AC}$ and $\vec{AD}$.
    We found it can be small.
    But maybe $C$ and $D$ are constrained such that they cannot be far in z?
    "Moving point C to line AB distance 2".
    "Moving point D to line AB distance 1".
    No constraint on z.
    So A is false.

    Maybe "Dihedral angle C-AB-D" constrains z?
    No.

    So, confident in B and C.

    Let's quickly check if there is any interpretation of "Dihedral angle" that involves the projection of C and D on the line AB?
    No.

    Let's double check calculation of B again.
    $C = (2, 0, z_C)$.
    $D = (1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, z_D)$.
    $CD^2 = (2-0.5)^2 + (0-\sqrt{3}/2)^2 + (z_C-z_D)^2$
    $= 1.5^2 + 0.75 + \dots$
    $= 2.25 + 0.75 = 3$.
    Yes.
    If angle was different, say $\alpha$.
    $CD^2 = 2^2 + 1^2 - 2(2)(1)\cos \alpha + \Delta z^2 = 5 - 4\cos \alpha + \Delta z^2$.
    Min value for $\alpha=60$ is 3.
    If $\alpha=60$, $\cos \alpha = 0.5$, $5-2=3$.
    If $\alpha$ could be different?
    Dihedral angle is fixed at 60.
    So $\alpha=60$.
    So min is 3.
    So B is True.

    Check C again.
    Condition $AB \perp CD \implies \Delta z = 0$.
    Then $CD^2 = 3$.
    Vector $\vec{CD}$ connects $(2,0)$ to $(0.5, \sqrt{3}/2)$.
    Slope $\frac{\sqrt{3}/2}{-1.5} = -\frac{\sqrt{3}}{3} = -1/\sqrt{3}$.
    Angle of $CD$ vector is $150^\circ$ (since $x<0, y>0$).
    Vector $OD$ angle is $60^\circ$.
    Angle between $CD$ and $OD$ is $150 - 60 = 90^\circ$.
    So $CD \perp OD$.
    Since $OD$ is in plane $ABD$ (horizontal projection), and $CD$ is horizontal, $CD \perp$ plane containing vertical and $OD$.
    Plane $ABD$ contains vertical (AB) and $D$ (so $OD$).
    So $CD \perp$ Plane $ABD$.
    Correct.

    Check D again.
    $AB \perp$ Plane $ACD$.
    Plane $ACD$ must be horizontal.
    So $C, D$ at height 0 (relative to A).
    Angle between $AC$ and $AD$ is angle between $(2,0)$ and $(0.5, \sqrt{3}/2)$.
    Angle is 60.
    $AC$ not perp to $AD$.
    Correct.

    What if $A$ is not origin?
    Just translation.
    Vectors $\vec{AC}$ and $\vec{AD}$ change, but angle $\angle CAD$ remains same.
    Distance $CD$ remains same.
    Perpendicularity remains same.

    So answers B, C.

gaokao_000_010 ✓ BCD BCD 10308 104.0 99.1 http://192.168.0.41:9292
Prompt
请解答以下数学题（多选题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

11. 已知圆C_1:(x+1)^2+y^2=1，圆C_2:(x-1)^2+y^2=1，圆C_3:x^2+(y-\sqrt{3})^2=1，直线l:y=kx+b与C_1, C_2, C_3均有两个交点。记l被C_1, C_2, C_3截得的弦长分别为s_1、s_2、s_3，则（ ）
A. k可以取任意实数
B. 满足s_1=s_2=s_3的直线l共有3条
C. 满足s_1+s_2+s_3=3的直线l多于3条
D. 当b=0时，s_1+s_2+s_3的最大值为\frac{2\sqrt{21}}{3}

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
**逐步推理：**

1. **基础几何分析**
   - 圆 $C_1, C_2, C_3$ 的半径均为 $r=1$。
   - 圆心坐标分别为 $O_1(-1,0), O_2(1,0), O_3(0,\sqrt{3})$。计算可知 $|O_1O_2|=|O_2O_3|=|O_3O_1|=2$，故 $\triangle O_1O_2O_3$ 为边长为 $2$ 的等边三角形。
   - 直线 $l: kx-y+b=0$ 到圆心 $O_i$ 的距离为 $d_i$。由弦长公式，$s_i = 2\sqrt{r^2-d_i^2} = 2\sqrt{1-d_i^2}$。
   - 直线与圆有两个交点的充要条件为 $0 \le d_i < 1 \ (i=1,2,3)$。

2. **选项A分析**
   - 由相交条件可得：$\frac{|b-k|}{\sqrt{k^2+1}}<1,\ \frac{|b+k|}{\sqrt{k^2+1}}<1,\ \frac{|b-\sqrt{3}|}{\sqrt{k^2+1}}<1$。
   - 联立前两个不等式得 $b \in (-\sqrt{k^2+1}+|k|,\ \sqrt{k^2+1}-|k|)$。
   - 联立该区间与第三个不等式 $b \in (\sqrt{3}-\sqrt{k^2+1},\ \sqrt{3}+\sqrt{k^2+1})$ 非空的条件为：
     $\sqrt{3} < (\sqrt{k^2+1}-|k|) + \sqrt{k^2+1} \Rightarrow 2\sqrt{k^2+1} > \sqrt{3}+|k|$。
   - 两边平方整理得 $3k^2 - 2\sqrt{3}|k| + 1 > 0 \Rightarrow (\sqrt{3}|k|-1)^2 > 0$。
   - 解得 $|k| \neq \frac{\sqrt{3}}{3}$，即 $k \neq \pm\frac{\sqrt{3}}{3}$。当 $k=\pm\frac{\sqrt{3}}{3}$ 时，直线与 $C_3$ 相切（只有一个交点），不满足题意。故 $k$ 不能取任意实数，**A错误**。

3. **选项B分析**
   - $s_1=s_2=s_3 \Leftrightarrow d_1^2=d_2^2=d_3^2$。
   - 由 $d_1^2=d_2^2 \Rightarrow (b-k)^2=(b+k)^2 \Rightarrow bk=0$，分两种情况：
     - 若 $b=0$，则 $\frac{k^2}{k^2+1} = \frac{3}{k^2+1} \Rightarrow k^2=3 \Rightarrow k=\pm\sqrt{3}$。此时 $d_1=d_2=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}<1$，满足条件，得2条直线。
     - 若 $k=0$，则 $b^2=(b-\sqrt{3})^2 \Rightarrow b=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}$。此时 $d_1=d_2=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}<1$，满足条件，得1条直线。
   - 综上，满足 $s_1=s_2=s_3$ 的直线共有 $2+1=3$ 条，**B正确**。

4. **选项C分析**
   - 设 $f(k,b) = s_1+s_2+s_3 = 2\sum_{i=1}^3 \sqrt{1-d_i^2}$。该函数在满足相交条件的 $(k,b)$ 平面区域上是连续函数。
   - 当直线远离圆心时，$d_i \to 1$，$f \to 0$；当直线经过三个圆心附近时，$d_i \to 0$，$f \to 6$。
   - 由介值定理，$f(k,b)=3$ 的解集构成参数平面上一条连续曲线，包含无穷多条直线。因此“多于3条”显然成立，**C正确**。

5. **选项D分析**
   - 当 $b=0$ 时，$d_1=d_2=\frac{|k|}{\sqrt{k^2+1}}$，$d_3=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{\sqrt{k^2+1}}$。
   - 相交条件要求 $d_3<1 \Rightarrow k^2>2$。令 $t=\sqrt{k^2+1}$，则 $t>\sqrt{3}$，且 $k^2=t^2-1$。
   - 弦长之和 $S(t) = 2\sqrt{1-\frac{t^2-1}{t^2}} \times 2 + 2\sqrt{1-\frac{3}{t^2}} = \frac{4}{t} + \frac{2\sqrt{t^2-3}}{t} = \frac{2(2+\sqrt{t^2-3})}{t}$。
   - 求导：$S'(t) = 2 \cdot \frac{\frac{t^2}{\sqrt{t^2-3}} - (2+\sqrt{t^2-3})}{t^2} = \frac{2}{t^2} \left( \frac{3}{\sqrt{t^2-3}} - 2 \right)$。
   - 令 $S'(t)=0$ 得 $\sqrt{t^2-3}=\frac{3}{2} \Rightarrow t^2=\frac{21}{4} \Rightarrow t=\frac{\sqrt{21}}{2} > \sqrt{3}$，在定义域内。
   - 此时 $S_{\max} = \frac{2(2+\frac{3}{2})}{\frac{\sqrt{21}}{2}} = \frac{14}{\sqrt{21}} = \frac{2\sqrt{21}}{3}$。**D正确**。

综上，正确选项为 B、C、D。

\boxed{BCD}
Reasoning
Here's a thinking process:

1.  **Understand the User Input:**
    - **Problem Type:** Multiple-choice question (possibly multiple correct answers).
    - **Given:**
        - Circle $C_1: (x+1)^2 + y^2 = 1$ (Center $(-1, 0)$, Radius $r_1=1$)
        - Circle $C_2: (x-1)^2 + y^2 = 1$ (Center $(1, 0)$, Radius $r_2=1$)
        - Circle $C_3: x^2 + (y-\sqrt{3})^2 = 1$ (Center $(0, \sqrt{3})$, Radius $r_3=1$)
        - Line $l: y = kx + b$ intersects each circle at two points.
        - Chord lengths: $s_1, s_2, s_3$ for $C_1, C_2, C_3$ respectively.
    - **Options:**
        - A: $k$ can be any real number.
        - B: There are exactly 3 lines $l$ such that $s_1 = s_2 = s_3$.
        - C: There are more than 3 lines $l$ such that $s_1 + s_2 + s_3 = 3$.
        - D: When $b=0$, the maximum value of $s_1 + s_2 + s_3$ is $\frac{2\sqrt{21}}{3}$.
    - **Task:** Solve step-by-step and box the final answer(s).

2.  **Analyze the Geometry & Conditions:**
    - All three circles have radius $r=1$.
    - Chord length formula: For a circle of radius $r$ and distance from center to line $d$, chord length $s = 2\sqrt{r^2 - d^2}$. Since $r=1$, $s = 2\sqrt{1 - d^2}$.
    - Condition "intersects at two points" means $d < 1$ for each circle.
    - Let $d_1, d_2, d_3$ be distances from centers $(-1,0), (1,0), (0,\sqrt{3})$ to line $l: kx - y + b = 0$.
    - $d_1 = \frac{|-k - 0 + b|}{\sqrt{k^2+1}} = \frac{|b-k|}{\sqrt{k^2+1}}$
    - $d_2 = \frac{|k - 0 + b|}{\sqrt{k^2+1}} = \frac{|b+k|}{\sqrt{k^2+1}}$
    - $d_3 = \frac{|0 - \sqrt{3} + b|}{\sqrt{k^2+1}} = \frac{|b-\sqrt{3}|}{\sqrt{k^2+1}}$
    - Chord lengths: $s_i = 2\sqrt{1 - d_i^2}$ for $i=1,2,3$.
    - Condition for two intersections: $0 \le d_i < 1$.

3.  **Evaluate Option A: $k$ can be any real number.**
    - Is there any restriction on $k$?
    - The line must intersect all three circles. This imposes conditions on $b$ relative to $k$.
    - But the option says "$k$ can be any real number", meaning for *any* given $k$, does there exist a $b$ such that the line intersects all three circles? Or does it mean in the context of the problem, $k$ is unconstrained? Usually, it means "is $k$ allowed to be any real number in the set of valid lines?"
    - Let's check if for any $k$, we can find $b$ such that $d_i < 1$ for $i=1,2,3$.
    - The centers form an equilateral triangle? Let's check distances between centers:
        - $O_1(-1,0), O_2(1,0) \Rightarrow |O_1O_2| = 2$.
        - $O_1(-1,0), O_3(0,\sqrt{3}) \Rightarrow |O_1O_3| = \sqrt{1^2 + (\sqrt{3})^2} = 2$.
        - $O_2(1,0), O_3(0,\sqrt{3}) \Rightarrow |O_2O_3| = \sqrt{(-1)^2 + (\sqrt{3})^2} = 2$.
    - Yes, centers form an equilateral triangle with side length 2.
    - The circles are mutually externally tangent? Distance between centers is 2, radius is 1, so they touch externally. Actually, $|O_1O_2|=2=r_1+r_2$, so $C_1$ and $C_2$ touch at $(0,0)$. Similarly, $C_1$ and $C_3$ touch at $(-1/2, \sqrt{3}/2)$, and $C_2$ and $C_3$ touch at $(1/2, \sqrt{3}/2)$.
    - For a line to cut all three circles, it must pass through the "interior" region near the origin? Actually, the three circles form a Reuleaux triangle-like gap in the middle, but they only touch at points. Wait, if they only touch, a line can pass through all three only if it goes through the central region. But since they only touch, the common interior region is empty. However, the line doesn't need to go through the interior of all three simultaneously in a connected way, it just needs to intersect each.
    - Let's check the distance from the centroid of the triangle to the sides. The centroid is $(0, \sqrt{3}/3)$.
    - Actually, consider $b=0$. Line $y=kx$. Distance to $(0,0)$ is 0. Distance to $O_3(0,\sqrt{3})$ is $\frac{|\sqrt{3}|}{\sqrt{k^2+1}}$. For this to be $<1$, $\sqrt{3} < \sqrt{k^2+1} \Rightarrow k^2 > 2 \Rightarrow |k| > \sqrt{2}$. So for $b=0$, we need $|k| > \sqrt{2}$.
    - What if we choose $b$ appropriately? The problem likely implies $k$ is a parameter of the line, and we are looking at the set of all valid lines. Does $k$ have restrictions?
    - Consider the line passing through the origin with slope $k$. It intersects $C_1$ and $C_2$ if distance to $O_1, O_2$ is $<1$. Distance to $(-1,0)$ is $\frac{|k|}{\sqrt{k^2+1}} < 1$, always true. Distance to $(1,0)$ is same. So $C_1, C_2$ are always cut by any line through origin. But $C_3$ requires $\frac{|\sqrt{3}|}{\sqrt{k^2+1}} < 1 \Rightarrow |k| > \sqrt{2}$.
    - What if we shift $b$? We need $|b-k| < \sqrt{k^2+1}$, $|b+k| < \sqrt{k^2+1}$, $|b-\sqrt{3}| < \sqrt{k^2+1}$.
    - This system of inequalities defines a region in $(k,b)$ plane. Is $k$ bounded?
    - From $|b-k| < \sqrt{k^2+1}$ and $|b+k| < \sqrt{k^2+1}$, we get constraints on $b$. Specifically, $b$ must be in $(-\sqrt{k^2+1}+k, \sqrt{k^2+1}+k) \cap (-\sqrt{k^2+1}-k, \sqrt{k^2+1}-k)$. The intersection is $(-\sqrt{k^2+1} + |k|, \sqrt{k^2+1} - |k|)$. For this interval to be non-empty, we need $\sqrt{k^2+1} - |k| > 0$, which is always true. So for any $k$, there is a non-empty range of $b$ satisfying conditions for $C_1$ and $C_2$.
    - Now add condition for $C_3$: $|b-\sqrt{3}| < \sqrt{k^2+1} \Rightarrow b \in (\sqrt{3}-\sqrt{k^2+1}, \sqrt{3}+\sqrt{k^2+1})$.
    - We need the intersection of $I_1 = (-\sqrt{k^2+1} + |k|, \sqrt{k^2+1} - |k|)$ and $I_3 = (\sqrt{3}-\sqrt{k^2+1}, \sqrt{3}+\sqrt{k^2+1})$ to be non-empty.
    - Actually, $I_1$ is centered at 0 with radius $\sqrt{k^2+1}-|k|$. $I_3$ is centered at $\sqrt{3}$ with radius $\sqrt{k^2+1}$.
    - The distance between centers is $\sqrt{3}$. For intersection, we need $\sqrt{3} < (\sqrt{k^2+1}-|k|) + \sqrt{k^2+1} = 2\sqrt{k^2+1} - |k|$.
    - So $2\sqrt{k^2+1} > \sqrt{3} + |k|$.
    - Square both sides: $4(k^2+1) > 3 + k^2 + 2\sqrt{3}|k| \Rightarrow 3k^2 + 1 > 2\sqrt{3}|k|$.
    - Let $u = |k| \ge 0$. $3u^2 - 2\sqrt{3}u + 1 > 0 \Rightarrow (\sqrt{3}u - 1)^2 > 0$.
    - This is true for all $u \neq 1/\sqrt{3}$. At $u=1/\sqrt{3}$, equality holds, interval touches at one point (distance exactly 1, tangent, not two points). But we need strict inequality for two intersection points. So $|k| \neq 1/\sqrt{3}$.
    - Thus, $k$ can be any real number except $k = \pm 1/\sqrt{3}$. So $k$ is *almost* any real number, but strictly speaking, there are two values excluded. In multiple choice questions, "can take any real number" usually means "is not restricted to a specific bounded range" or "the set of possible k is $\mathbb{R}$". Given the strict condition for two points, maybe it's considered true in the context, or maybe false because of the two points. I'll mark it as likely True or False depending on standard conventions. Often, "任意实数" means no upper/lower bound. I'll lean towards True, but will double-check later. Actually, the condition $(\sqrt{3}|k|-1)^2 > 0$ means $k \neq \pm \frac{\sqrt{3}}{3}$. So technically not *all* reals. But maybe the question implies "no restriction on magnitude" or it's considered true. I'll keep it as potentially true. Let's check other options first.

4.  **Evaluate Option B: $s_1=s_2=s_3 \Rightarrow$ 3 lines.**
    - $s_1=s_2=s_3 \iff d_1^2=d_2^2=d_3^2$.
    - $d_1^2 = d_2^2 \Rightarrow (b-k)^2 = (b+k)^2 \Rightarrow -2bk = 2bk \Rightarrow 4bk=0 \Rightarrow b=0$ or $k=0$.
    - Case 1: $b=0$. Then $d_1=d_2=\frac{|k|}{\sqrt{k^2+1}}$. Condition $d_1^2=d_3^2 \Rightarrow \frac{k^2}{k^2+1} = \frac{3}{k^2+1} \Rightarrow k^2=3 \Rightarrow k=\pm\sqrt{3}$.
        - Lines: $y=\sqrt{3}x$ and $y=-\sqrt{3}x$. Check intersection condition: $d_1 = \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} < 1$. Valid. Two lines.
    - Case 2: $k=0$. Then $d_1=d_2=|b|$. Condition $d_1^2=d_3^2 \Rightarrow b^2 = (b-\sqrt{3})^2 \Rightarrow b^2 = b^2 - 2\sqrt{3}b + 3 \Rightarrow 2\sqrt{3}b=3 \Rightarrow b=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}$.
        - Line: $y=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}$. Check $d_1 = \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} < 1$. Valid. One line.
    - Total lines: 3.
    - So Option B is **True**.

5.  **Evaluate Option C: $s_1+s_2+s_3=3 \Rightarrow$ more than 3 lines.**
    - $s_i = 2\sqrt{1-d_i^2}$. Let $s_1+s_2+s_3=3 \Rightarrow \sqrt{1-d_1^2} + \sqrt{1-d_2^2} + \sqrt{1-d_3^2} = \frac{3}{2}$.
    - Since $d_i < 1$, $\sqrt{1-d_i^2} \in (0, 1]$. The sum is at most 3. We want sum = 1.5.
    - This seems like a continuous range of lines. Symmetry suggests many solutions.
    - Let's check if $s_1+s_2+s_3$ is continuous and takes values around 1.5.
    - When line passes far away, $d_i \to 1$, $s_i \to 0$, sum $\to 0$.
    - When line is close to centers, $d_i$ small, $s_i$ close to 2, sum close to 6.
    - So by Intermediate Value Theorem, sum=3 is achievable.
    - Due to symmetry (reflection across y-axis, rotation by 120°? Wait, centers are equilateral, but line equation isn't rotationally symmetric in a simple way for fixed k,b. But geometrically, the setup is symmetric under 120° rotation about centroid $(0, \sqrt{3}/3)$? Let's check centers: $O_1(-1,0), O_2(1,0), O_3(0,\sqrt{3})$. Centroid $G(0, \sqrt{3}/3)$.
    - Rotate by 120° around G: $O_1 \to O_2 \to O_3 \to O_1$. The circles are identical. So the problem is rotationally symmetric about G.
    - If line $L$ cuts all three with chords $s_1, s_2, s_3$, then rotating $L$ by 120° gives another line $L'$ with chords permuted.
    - If $s_1+s_2+s_3 = 3$ for one line, it holds for its rotations.
    - Also, if we reflect across the altitude from $O_3$ (y-axis), symmetry holds.
    - This symmetry implies if there's one solution, there are at least 3 (rotations). But the question says "more than 3". Given the continuous nature of the chord length sum function over the parameter space of lines intersecting all three, the level set $s_1+s_2+s_3=3$ is likely a curve or a set of curves in the $(k,b)$ plane, hence infinitely many lines. Wait, is it a curve? The space of lines intersecting all three is a 2D region. The condition $S=3$ defines a 1D curve within it. So infinitely many lines. "多于3条" (more than 3) is definitely true.
    - Let's verify carefully: The set of lines intersecting all three circles is an open region in the dual plane (or $(k,b)$ plane). The function $S(k,b) = s_1+s_2+s_3$ is continuous. If it takes values both above and below 3 in this region, the preimage $S=3$ is a 1D manifold (curve), containing infinitely many lines. So "more than 3" is trivially true.
    - Option C is **True**.

6.  **Evaluate Option D: $b=0 \Rightarrow \max(s_1+s_2+s_3) = \frac{2\sqrt{21}}{3}$.**
    - If $b=0$, line is $y=kx$.
    - $d_1 = d_2 = \frac{|k|}{\sqrt{k^2+1}}$.
    - $d_3 = \frac{\sqrt{3}}{\sqrt{k^2+1}}$.
    - Condition for two intersections: $d_3 < 1 \Rightarrow \frac{3}{k^2+1} < 1 \Rightarrow k^2 > 2$. Also $d_1 < 1$ is always true.
    - So $|k| > \sqrt{2}$. Let $k > \sqrt{2}$ (symmetry for $k<-\sqrt{2}$ gives same sum).
    - $s_1 = s_2 = 2\sqrt{1 - \frac{k^2}{k^2+1}} = 2\sqrt{\frac{1}{k^2+1}} = \frac{2}{\sqrt{k^2+1}}$.
    - $s_3 = 2\sqrt{1 - \frac{3}{k^2+1}} = 2\sqrt{\frac{k^2-2}{k^2+1}}$.
    - Sum $S(k) = s_1+s_2+s_3 = \frac{4}{\sqrt{k^2+1}} + \frac{2\sqrt{k^2-2}}{\sqrt{k^2+1}} = \frac{2}{\sqrt{k^2+1}}(2 + \sqrt{k^2-2})$.
    - Let $t = \sqrt{k^2+1}$. Since $k^2 > 2$, $t > \sqrt{3}$. Also $k^2 = t^2-1$.

Prompt
请解答以下数学题（填空题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

12. 双曲线5x^2 - 6y^2 = 1的离心率为__________.

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
将双曲线方程化为标准形式是解题的关键。具体步骤如下：

1. **化为标准方程**：
   给定方程为 $5x^2 - 6y^2 = 1$，两边同时除以 $1$ 可化为标准形式：
   $$\frac{x^2}{\frac{1}{5}} - \frac{y^2}{\frac{1}{6}} = 1$$

2. **确定 $a^2$ 与 $b^2$**：
   由于 $x^2$ 项的系数为正，焦点在 $x$ 轴上。根据标准方程 $\frac{x^2}{a^2} - \frac{y^2}{b^2} = 1$，可得：
   $$a^2 = \frac{1}{5}, \quad b^2 = \frac{1}{6}$$

3. **计算半焦距 $c$ 的平方**：
   双曲线中满足关系式 $c^2 = a^2 + b^2$，代入数值：
   $$c^2 = \frac{1}{5} + \frac{1}{6} = \frac{6}{30} + \frac{5}{30} = \frac{11}{30}$$

4. **计算离心率 $e$**：
   离心率公式为 $e = \frac{c}{a} = \sqrt{\frac{c^2}{a^2}}$，代入 $c^2$ 和 $a^2$：
   $$e = \sqrt{\frac{\frac{11}{30}}{\frac{1}{5}}} = \sqrt{\frac{11}{30} \times 5} = \sqrt{\frac{11}{6}}$$
   分母有理化得：
   $$e = \frac{\sqrt{11 \times 6}}{6} = \frac{\sqrt{66}}{6}$$

最终答案为：$\boxed{\frac{\sqrt{66}}{6}}$
Reasoning
Here's a thinking process:

1.  **Analyze User Input:**
   - **Question:** 双曲线 $5x^2 - 6y^2 = 1$ 的离心率为__________.
   - **Type:** Fill-in-the-blank math problem.
   - **Requirement:** Step-by-step reasoning, final answer in $\boxed{}$.

2.  **Identify Key Mathematical Concepts:**
   - The given equation is $5x^2 - 6y^2 = 1$.
   - This is a hyperbola.
   - Standard form of a hyperbola: $\frac{x^2}{a^2} - \frac{y^2}{b^2} = 1$ (opens left/right) or $\frac{y^2}{a^2} - \frac{x^2}{b^2} = 1$ (opens up/down).
   - For $\frac{x^2}{a^2} - \frac{y^2}{b^2} = 1$, $a^2 > 0$, $b^2 > 0$.
   - Relationship: $c^2 = a^2 + b^2$, where $c$ is the distance from center to focus.
   - Eccentricity: $e = \frac{c}{a}$.
   - Need to convert the given equation to standard form to find $a^2$ and $b^2$.

3.  **Convert Equation to Standard Form:**
   - Given: $5x^2 - 6y^2 = 1$
   - Rewrite as: $\frac{x^2}{1/5} - \frac{y^2}{1/6} = 1$
   - So, $a^2 = \frac{1}{5}$, $b^2 = \frac{1}{6}$.
   - Note: Since the $x^2$ term is positive, it's a horizontal hyperbola. $a$ corresponds to the $x$-axis.

4.  **Calculate $c^2$ and $c$:**
   - $c^2 = a^2 + b^2 = \frac{1}{5} + \frac{1}{6}$
   - Find common denominator: $30$
   - $c^2 = \frac{6}{30} + \frac{5}{30} = \frac{11}{30}$
   - So, $c = \sqrt{\frac{11}{30}}$

5.  **Calculate Eccentricity $e$:**
   - $e = \frac{c}{a} = \frac{\sqrt{11/30}}{\sqrt{1/5}}$
   - Simplify: $e = \sqrt{\frac{11/30}{1/5}} = \sqrt{\frac{11}{30} \cdot 5} = \sqrt{\frac{11}{6}}$
   - Rationalize if necessary: $\sqrt{\frac{11}{6}} = \frac{\sqrt{66}}{6}$
   - Usually, $\sqrt{\frac{11}{6}}$ or $\frac{\sqrt{66}}{6}$ are acceptable. I'll stick with $\frac{\sqrt{66}}{6}$ or $\sqrt{\frac{11}{6}}$. Let's check standard conventions. Often, $\frac{\sqrt{66}}{6}$ is preferred.

   Let's double check the calculation:
   $a^2 = 1/5$, $b^2 = 1/6$
   $c^2 = a^2 + b^2 = 1/5 + 1/6 = 11/30$
   $e^2 = c^2 / a^2 = (11/30) / (1/5) = 11/6$
   $e = \sqrt{11/6} = \sqrt{66}/6$
   Correct.

6.  **Formulate Step-by-Step Reasoning:**
   - Step 1: Write the given equation in standard form: $\frac{x^2}{1/5} - \frac{y^2}{1/6} = 1$.
   - Step 2: Identify $a^2$ and $b^2$. Here, $a^2 = \frac{1}{5}$, $b^2 = \frac{1}{6}$.
   - Step 3: Use the relationship for hyperbolas: $c^2 = a^2 + b^2$. Calculate $c^2 = \frac{1}{5} + \frac{1}{6} = \frac{11}{30}$.
   - Step 4: Recall the formula for eccentricity: $e = \frac{c}{a} = \sqrt{\frac{c^2}{a^2}}$.
   - Step 5: Substitute values: $e = \sqrt{\frac{11/30}{1/5}} = \sqrt{\frac{11}{6}} = \frac{\sqrt{66}}{6}$.
   - Step 6: Conclude with the final answer.

   Check for potential pitfalls:
   - Confusing $a^2$ and $b^2$: The positive term corresponds to $a^2$. Here, $x^2$ is positive, so $a^2 = 1/5$. Correct.
   - Formula for $e$: $e = c/a$ for hyperbola. Correct.
   - Calculation: $1/5 + 1/6 = 11/30$. $(11/30)/(1/5) = 11/6$. Square root is $\sqrt{11/6} = \sqrt{66}/6$. Correct.

   All steps are solid.

   $$\frac{x^2}{\frac{1}{5}} - \frac{y^2}{\frac{1}{6}} = 1$$

   $$a^2 = \frac{1}{5}, \quad b^2 = \frac{1}{6}$$
   对于双曲线，半焦距 $c$ 满足关系式 $c^2 = a^2 + b^2$，代入得：
   $$c^2 = \frac{1}{5} + \frac{1}{6} = \frac{6+5}{30} = \frac{11}{30}$$

gaokao_000_012 ✓ \frac{3\pi}{2}, 1 \frac{3\pi}{2}, 1 20174 103.8 194.4 http://192.168.0.41:9292
Prompt
请解答以下数学题（填空题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

13. 已知f(x)=2sin(ax+\theta) (a \in Z, 0 \le \theta < 2\pi)是偶函数，f(x)在区间(0, \frac{\pi}{2})单调递增。则\theta =__________，f(\frac{2\pi}{3}) =__________.

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
**第一步：确定参数 $\theta$ 的值**
1.  **利用偶函数性质：**
    因为 $f(x)$ 是偶函数，所以 $f(-x) = f(x)$。
    即 $2\sin(-ax + \theta) = 2\sin(ax + \theta)$。
    根据三角恒等式，上式等价于 $\cos\theta = 0$。
    结合题目给定的范围 $0 \le \theta < 2\pi$，$\theta$ 的可能取值为 $\frac{\pi}{2}$ 或 $\frac{3\pi}{2}$。

2.  **利用单调递增性质筛选：**
    *   **情形 1：** 当 $\theta = \frac{\pi}{2}$ 时，$f(x) = 2\cos(ax)$。
        由于 $f(0)=2$ 是函数的最大值，函数在 $x=0$ 右侧必然单调递减，这与题目要求的“在 $(0, \frac{\pi}{2})$ 单调递增”矛盾，故舍去。
    *   **情形 2：** 当 $\theta = \frac{3\pi}{2}$ 时，$f(x) = -2\cos(ax)$。
        此时 $f(0)=-2$，在区间 $(0, \frac{\pi}{2})$ 上有可能单调递增。
        因此，确定 $\theta = \frac{3\pi}{2}$。

**第二步：确定 $a$ 的取值并计算函数值**
1.  **分析 $a$ 的取值范围：**
    函数为 $f(x) = -2\cos(ax)$。要使其在 $(0, \frac{\pi}{2})$ 上单调递增，导数 $f'(x) = 2a\sin(ax)$ 在该区间内需恒大于 0。
    这意味着 $a$ 的绝对值必须满足 $|a| \cdot \frac{\pi}{2} \le \pi$，即 $|a| \le 2$。
    因为 $a \in \mathbb{Z}$ 且 $a \neq 0$（否则为常数函数），所以 $a \in \{1, 2, -1, -2\}$。

2.  **计算 $f(\frac{2\pi}{3})$：**
    代入 $f(x) = -2\cos(ax)$ 和 $x = \frac{2\pi}{3}$：
    $$f\left(\frac{2\pi}{3}\right) = -2\cos\left(\frac{2\pi a}{3}\right)$$
    由于 $a$ 的取值为 $\pm 1$ 或 $\pm 2$（即 $a$ 不是 3 的倍数），则 $\frac{2\pi a}{3}$ 的终边落在第三或第二象限的角平分线附近，其余弦值恒为 $-\frac{1}{2}$：
    *   若 $a=\pm 1$，$\cos(\pm \frac{2\pi}{3}) = -\frac{1}{2}$。
    *   若 $a=\pm 2$，$\cos(\pm \frac{4\pi}{3}) = -\frac{1}{2}$。
    代入计算得：
    $$f\left(\frac{2\pi}{3}\right) = -2 \times \left(-\frac{1}{2}\right) = 1$$

**结论**
$\theta = \frac{3\pi}{2}$，$f(\frac{2\pi}{3}) = 1$。

\boxed{\frac{3\pi}{2}, 1}

Here's a thinking process to solve the problem:

1.  **Understand the Goal:** The user wants me to solve a specific math problem (a fill-in-the-blank question) involving a trigonometric function. I need to find the value of $\theta$ and the value of $f(\frac{2\pi}{3})$.

2.  **Analyze the Given Information:**
    *   Function: $f(x) = 2\sin(ax + \theta)$
    *   Parameters: $a \in \mathbb{Z}$ (integer), $0 \le \theta < 2\pi$.
    *   Property 1: $f(x)$ is an even function (偶函数).
    *   Property 2: $f(x)$ is monotonically increasing (单调递增) on the interval $(0, \frac{\pi}{2})$.

3.  **Step-by-Step Deduction:**

    *   **Step 1: Use the "Even Function" property.**
        *   Definition of an even function: $f(-x) = f(x)$ for all $x$.
        *   Substitute into the function expression:
            $2\sin(a(-x) + \theta) = 2\sin(ax + \theta)$
            $\sin(-ax + \theta) = \sin(ax + \theta)$
        *   Using the sine addition/subtraction formulas:
            $\sin\theta \cos(ax) - \cos\theta \sin(ax) = \sin\theta \cos(ax) + \cos\theta \sin(ax)$
        *   Subtract $\sin\theta \cos(ax)$ from both sides:
            $-\cos\theta \sin(ax) = \cos\theta \sin(ax)$
            $2\cos\theta \sin(ax) = 0$
        *   For this to hold for *all* $x$ (where $a \neq 0$ implies $\sin(ax)$ varies), we must have $\cos\theta = 0$.
        *   If $a=0$, $f(x) = 2\sin(\theta)$ is a constant, which is even. But let's check the interval condition later. Usually, in these problems $a \neq 0$. If $a=0$, $f(x)$ is constant, not strictly increasing, so $a \neq 0$.
        *   So, $\cos\theta = 0$.
        *   Given the constraint $0 \le \theta < 2\pi$, the possible values for $\theta$ are $\frac{\pi}{2}$ and $\frac{3\pi}{2}$.
        *   Let's check the function forms:
            *   Case 1: $\theta = \frac{\pi}{2}$. Then $f(x) = 2\sin(ax + \frac{\pi}{2}) = 2\cos(ax)$.
            *   Case 2: $\theta = \frac{3\pi}{2}$. Then $f(x) = 2\sin(ax + \frac{3\pi}{2}) = -2\cos(ax)$.

    *   **Step 2: Use the "Monotonicity" property.**
        *   We are looking at the interval $(0, \frac{\pi}{2})$.
        *   The function must be *monotonically increasing* here.

        *   **Analyze Case 1: $f(x) = 2\cos(ax)$**
            *   Derivative: $f'(x) = -2a\sin(ax)$.
            *   For $f(x)$ to be increasing on $(0, \frac{\pi}{2})$, we need $f'(x) \ge 0$.
            *   $-2a\sin(ax) \ge 0$ for $x \in (0, \frac{\pi}{2})$.
            *   Since $x \in (0, \frac{\pi}{2})$ is positive, let's look at signs.
            *   If $a > 0$:
                *   $\sin(ax)$ needs to be $\le 0$ for $x \in (0, \frac{\pi}{2})$.
                *   Since $x > 0$ and $a > 0$, $ax > 0$. The sine of a positive number is positive (until $\pi$). It cannot be $\le 0$ immediately near 0 unless $a=0$ (ruled out) or specific intervals. But for $x$ close to 0, $ax$ is close to 0, so $\sin(ax) > 0$. Then $-2a\sin(ax) < 0$.
                *   So $f(x)$ would be decreasing near 0. Thus, $a$ cannot be a positive integer.
            *   If $a < 0$: Let $a = -k$ where $k \in \mathbb{Z}^+$.
                *   $f(x) = 2\cos(-kx) = 2\cos(kx)$ (Cosine is even).
                *   $f'(x) = -2(-k)\sin(kx) = 2k\sin(kx)$.
                *   We need $2k\sin(kx) \ge 0$ for $x \in (0, \frac{\pi}{2})$.
                *   Since $k > 0$, we need $\sin(kx) \ge 0$.
                *   For small positive $x$, $\sin(kx) > 0$ provided $kx < \pi$.
                *   The interval is $(0, \frac{\pi}{2})$. So we need $k(\frac{\pi}{2}) \le \pi$ (or just $\sin$ stays positive).
                *   Actually, $\sin(y) > 0$ for $y \in (0, \pi)$.
                *   We need $kx \in (0, k\frac{\pi}{2})$ to be inside a region where sine is non-negative.
                *   The simplest condition is that the interval $(0, \frac{\pi}{2})$ maps to $(0, \text{something} \le \pi)$.
                *   So, $k \cdot \frac{\pi}{2} \le \pi \implies k \le 2$.
                *   Possible integer values for $k$: 1, 2.
                *   Since $a = -k$, possible $a$: $-1, -2$.
                *   Let's check specific values.
                    *   If $a = -1$: $f(x) = 2\cos(-x) = 2\cos(x)$. Is it increasing on $(0, \pi/2)$?
                        *   Graph of $\cos(x)$ decreases on $(0, \pi)$. So $2\cos(x)$ is decreasing. Wait.
                        *   Derivative check again: $f'(x) = -2(-1)\sin(-x)$? No.
                        *   $f(x) = 2\cos(-x) = 2\cos(x)$. $f'(x) = -2\sin(x)$. For $x \in (0, \pi/2)$, $\sin(x) > 0$, so $f'(x) < 0$. Decreasing.
                        *   Wait, why did the derivative logic suggest $a<0$ works?
                        *   Let's re-evaluate $f(x) = 2\cos(ax)$ with $a < 0$.
                        *   Let $a = -1$. $f(x) = 2\cos(-x) = 2\cos(x)$. Decreasing.

                            *   Wait, $\sin(-x) = -\sin(x)$.
                            *   $f(x) = 2\cos(-x) = 2\cos(x)$. Decreasing.
                            *   Is there any integer $a$ where $2\cos(ax)$ is increasing on $(0, \pi/2)$?
